# Passenger Agent OS — Progress

Append-only worklog. Every agent adds an entry when it finishes a task — what shipped, what it learned, what it left behind. **Newest first — insert your entry at the top, never append at end-of-file** (L-003). `BOARD.md` holds current state; this holds history.

Entry format:

```
### YYYY-MM-DD — <agent> — <task id> <short title>
- **Did:** what actually changed (files, migrations, issue IDs)
- **Evidence:** build/test output, screenshots, review verdicts
- **Left behind:** follow-ups, known gaps, anything the next agent needs
```

Read the PRD and TRD in full before touching source, then checked every non-trivial claim against `passenger-code` directly rather than trusting the TRD's own citations.
- **Confirmed sound, independently, before finding anyone else's review:** `PlaceDetailModal.swift:76-86`'s `touristTrapSlot` is exactly the reserved `EmptyView()` C7 targets. `HoodSheet.swift` (read in full) has no flag surface of any kind — D5's premise is real, not assumed. D3's reconciled reading (identical set of stroked Hoods at every hour, only style varies on an already-flagged one) matches `map-rendering-spec.md` line 42-43 verbatim ("Flagged + busy... replaces the plain flagged stroke outright" against a busy threshold that is explicitly per-slider-hour) — a sound, correctly-flagged-to-`product` resolution of the PRD's req 2/req 5 contradiction, not an invented one. D4's `UIWindow` justification checked against the actual view tree: `MapScreen.swift` already composes `.overlay(...)` (×3) and `.sheet(isPresented:)` as sibling modifiers on the same chain (lines ~125-157) — a root overlay genuinely would sit behind an open sheet, confirming D4's premise is a real, present conflict in this codebase, not a hypothetical one; a passthrough `UIWindow` with `hitTest` returning `nil` outside its bounds is the standard, correct fix. `LocationStore.swift` holds only `authorizationStatus`, never a `CLLocation`; grepped the tree for `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription`/`UIBackgroundModes`/`UNUserNotification` — zero hits on all three, matching §1's stubbed-detector claim exactly. All 24 bundled Hoods and all 9 bundled places confirmed `null` today by direct grep. `nameLabelSpanThreshold = 0.06` and the cold-open span `0.14` (`MapScreen.swift:13,18`) both confirmed, so D1's "new threshold strictly between 0.06 and 0.14" instruction is buildable as stated. Migration number `007` doesn't collide — `003`/`004`/`005`/`006` are each confirmed claimed by other in-flight TRDs (`hood-dataset`, `places-dataset`, `live-events-pipeline`, this task's own regenerated `006`), not merely asserted.
- **Mid-pass, found `ios-code-reviewer`'s own independent T-035 C1–C14 verdict already in this file (REQUEST CHANGES, three blocking findings) — re-derived each from source myself rather than accepting the write-up, per this environment's own standing discipline:**
  1. **`InstallIdentity` vs `analytics/EVENTS.md` — confirmed real.** Read `analytics/EVENTS.md`'s "Identity constraint" section directly: *"Every table keys on an anonymous `install_id` — **the same identifier `local_qa_answers` already uses**"*, with `app_installs.install_id` defined as client-generated and **persisted in SwiftData**. TRD §3.3's `InstallIdentity` is a separately-generated UUID living only in `local-qa.json` (Application Support JSON), deliberately walled off from anything `Hoods/`/`Map/`/catalog-adjacent per §2.2. Two independent generation paths cannot produce the same value — a genuine, undocumented cross-doc contract conflict, not a nitpick, since `EVENTS.md` predates this TRD by a day and already assumes the identifier this TRD independently invents.
  2. **§2.2's "no type holds both `LocalQAAnswerStore` and either catalog" — confirmed false as literally stated.** `MapScreen.swift:60` already holds `@State private var placeCatalog = PlaceCatalog()` and line 43 holds `hoods: [Hood]` (from `HoodCatalog`). All three findings are real once checked against source; nothing in my own pass contradicts them. ### 2026-08-02 — architect — T-036/PAS-27 TRD **v2**: the shared `MapChromeState` contract now says what T-032 actually specifies; both build orders safe

- **Did:** revised `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` to **v2** against the `trd-review` REQUEST CHANGES verdict — `ios-developer` (`passenger-brain 1002595`) and `ios-code-reviewer` (concurring independently, processed in `passenger-brain d3fa249`). Both entries read in full first. **No decision, contract or build step redesigned** — D1–D9 stand as written at v1; every edit is an accuracy fix, which is what the review asked for.
- **The blocking finding, fixed at its root.** v1 §4.5 declared `enum NavSurface: Equatable { case heat, places }` and §2.2 claimed an "identical contract, either build order" with T-032. It was not identical, and the "either order" promise was made unilaterally. **Read T-032's real §4.1 directly rather than trusting the brief** — both the committed version (`git show HEAD:prds/time-slider/TRD.md`) and the working-tree v3-in-flight version, which are identical in this section: `enum NavSurface: String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable { case search, heat, places, profile; var id: String { rawValue } }`, built **unconditionally** at T-032's C1 with no "skip if it exists" clause. Four cases vs. two, four conformances vs. one. So in the ordering the dispatch flagged as live (T-036 first), v1's C6 would have created a narrower stub that T-032's C1 then had to restructure — the exact outcome §2.2 claimed was impossible.
- **What v2 changes, five sites:** **§4.5** reproduces T-032 §4.1 verbatim (the `swift` block, plus a one-line provenance comment, is byte-identical — verified with a `diff` of the two extracted blocks, not by eye); **§2.2** is rewritten from "shared contract, both add a case" to the real one-directional dependency — T-032 owns the type and fully specifies it, T-036 is a consumer that **adds no case, no conformance, no method**; **C6** now reads "if `MapChromeState.swift` does not exist yet, create it exactly per T-032 §4.1 — all four cases, all four conformances, verbatim; if it exists, add nothing"; **§10's risk row** restated (v1's version of that row asserted the false claim); **§2.1's file list** marks the file NOT MODIFIED. §2.2 also names the residual risk the fix cannot remove — T-032 §4.1 changing after this was copied — and makes diffing the two an explicit re-review step.
- **Both build orders are now safe, which is the point:** T-036-first creates the real four-case type that T-032's C1 then finds already correct; T-032-first is a zero-edit reuse. There is only ever one shape of this type in the tree, so "whoever builds second must not restructure it" stops being a promise anyone has to keep.
- **Also folded in (non-blocking should-fix from `ios-code-reviewer`):** §3.4 now pins the **update** invariant the real Phase-2 detector inherits alongside the record shape — keep the higher `VisitKind` on a repeat observation, never overwrite, never append a second row. PRD req 3 bullet 4 ("revisiting a Been place changes no label") depends on nothing else, and it previously existed only as a Phase-1 fixture-authoring rule (§4.2/C4) that would vanish with the fixture.
- **Two edits owed to T-032's TRD were deliberately NOT made, and this is the reason:** `ios-code-reviewer` asked for a one-line correction to T-032's stale D1 prose ("the rest" of the nav buttons slot into `MapNavRow` — D7 corrects that for Places) and `ios-developer` recommended a symmetric no-op note on T-032's C1. Both live in `prds/time-slider/TRD.md`, which **had another session's uncommitted, partly-staged v3 work in the shared tree** when this pass ran (`git status`: `M ` staged). Editing or staging it would have swept that session's in-flight text into this commit and attributed it to this review — the precise `0dd3d21` failure `CLAUDE.md` rule 2 exists to stop. Left for T-032's own in-flight v3 pass, named in T-036 §10, in this entry, and in the BOARD row. **Neither blocks T-036** — after v2, T-036 creates nothing T-032 would have to restructure, so the symmetry is now a documentation nicety rather than a correctness dependency.
- **Evidence:** `diff` of the two extracted Swift blocks reported no difference; `git show HEAD:prds/time-slider/TRD.md` §4.1 and C1 checked against the working-tree copy (identical in that section); `git diff --staged` inspected before committing. No `passenger-code` changes — this is a document pass.
- **Left behind:** (1) **`agent-os/BOARD.md` is NOT in this commit.** Its T-036 rows carry this pass's readiness note in the working tree, but the file also holds **another session's staged T-032 row rewrite**, so committing it by path would have carried that hunk under this commit message. Rule 2 says unstage and name the owner: it belongs to the T-032 v3 `architect` session, and `project-manager` should confirm it lands rather than assuming (L-017). (2) T-036 is **ready for a `trd-review` re-run by both `ios-developer` and `ios-code-reviewer`** — `architect` did not flip the state; that is `chief-of-staff`'s. (3) The two T-032 TRD one-liners above. (4) Unchanged from v1 and not re-litigated here: the four items flagged for `product` (D4 sort, D5 no offline banner, D7 button placement and its fade consequence, §9 row 5's req-5 framing), and the ownerless shared dwell detector.

### 2026-08-02 — architect — T-032/PAS-15 TRD **v3**: all four `trd-review` findings resolved; found the changelog promising a fix the body never received

- **Did:** revised `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` to **v3** against the split `trd-review` verdict — `ios-developer` APPROVE WITH MINORS (`passenger-brain 27faac4`) and `ios-code-reviewer` REQUEST CHANGES (`passenger-brain 7a1f99c`). Both reviews read in full before touching the doc. **No decision, contract or build step redesigned** — D1–D10 all stand as written at v2; every v3 edit is a verification or accuracy fix, which is what both reviews asked for.
- **Blocking finding (`ios-code-reviewer`) — resolved.** §9 row 7 and C13 checked camera-immutability only for an inert horizontal drag (7b) and a pan starting *outside* the band (7c), never for a normal **in-band vertical** slide — the gesture the task exists to ship. Both v2 cases pass whether or not MapKit saw the touch, so D7/§2.4's central hit-test claim had no falsifiable check anywhere. Now: **§9 row 7 gains sub-check (e)** — `selectedHour` sweeps all 13 stops while `camera`/`MKCoordinateRegion` stays byte-identical, sampled at touch-down, mid-drag and `onEnded`, using the same comparison row 2c already uses, on device or in a UI test. **C13 is redefined as not-done until that check passes**, so it fails at build rather than at `qa` or after ship. §2.4, §4.8 rule (a) and D7 now state the hit-test claim as a *prediction this TRD requires confirmed*, not settled fact — the reviewer's point that the doc's own `FB19394663` citation is evidence against taking it on reasoning alone is correct and is now written into the doc. A matching risk row was added to §10 naming the real fallback: if it fails there is no third construction (`Map` exposes no recognizer to arbitrate against), so it becomes a product conversation about the edge path.
- **Three minors — all resolved.** (1) `coordinateSpace: .named("map")` → the default **`.local`**, with the reason recorded so it isn't re-invented at build time; **verified myself, not taken from the review** — `grep -rn "coordinateSpace" passenger-code/Passenger/` returns zero hits, so the named space had no declaring view anywhere. (2) §9 row 6's Step column now reads **`C4` (the ≥44pt frame), C9, C12** so `qa`'s `TEST-PLAN.md` traces to where the assertion is actually built. (3) row 7d / C13's sheet check now exercises **both detents and both touch targets** — four combinations, not one — after confirming at source that `MapScreen.swift:186` sets `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))`, `HoodSheet.swift:26` carries `[.medium, .large]`, and `PlaceDetailModal.swift:22` is `.medium`-only (so `.large` is reachable through the Hood sheet alone). `EdgeAvailability` keeps its one flat rule — this is verification granularity, not a design change.
- **Fifth finding, mine, raised by neither review:** the TRD named the shipped sheet **`HoodStubSheet`** in three places (§1, §9 row 7, §11 footer). **No such type exists** — `Detail/HoodSheet.swift:7` declares `HoodSheet` and `HoodStubSheet` appears nowhere in `passenger-code`. Left standing it would have pointed `ios-developer` and `qa` at a type they cannot find, in the very rows this pass was fixing. Renamed at all three sites; name only, no behaviour or scope change.
- **Shared-working-tree finding, stated plainly rather than left to the diff (rule 2).** When this pass opened the file, the v3 header, the four-row changelog table and the §2.4 / §4.8 / D7 / §9 edits were **already present in the tree, uncommitted** — not this pass's writing, and `TRD.md`'s mtime advanced again mid-pass, so at least one other session was writing the same file concurrently. I did not assume that work was correct and I did not re-do it: each piece was re-read against `27faac4`, `7a1f99c` and the shipped source before being allowed to stand. **Doing that caught a real defect: the changelog claimed "C13's verify-list gains the matching line" and "C13 now exercises both detents" — and C13 was untouched v2 text.** A doc asserting fixes it does not contain would have cleared re-review on its own summary. What this pass actually wrote: §4.10's detent note, C13's full verify-list, §10's risk row, §2.4's forward pointer, finding 5, and a note in the changelog telling a re-reviewer which parts were pre-existing so they get checked rather than trusted.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn "coordinateSpace\|\.named(" passenger-code/Passenger/` → 0 hits; `MapScreen.swift:186`, `HoodSheet.swift:26`, `PlaceDetailModal.swift:22` read directly for the detent split; `grep -rn "struct HoodSheet\|HoodStubSheet" passenger-code/Passenger/` → only `HoodSheet.swift:7`. Post-edit checks: zero live `coordinateSpace: .named` prescriptions (the two remaining mentions are the changelog and §4.8 describing the corrected-away value), zero `HoodStubSheet` outside the changelog row that documents the rename, row 6 Step column reads `C4, C9, C12`.
- **Left behind:** **T-032 is ready for `trd-review` re-run** — both `ios-developer` and `ios-code-reviewer` again, since both signoffs are required and one was a block. **I did not flip the board state** (`chief-of-staff`'s). Unchanged and still open from v2, none of it touched by this pass: D9's landscape exclusion, D10's seed and D7's two accepted costs all still need **`product`**; the non-whole-hour-timezone `[ASSUMPTION]` (§3.2); req 2's "any open sheet is unchanged" still satisfied structurally rather than behaviourally (D4/Q6); D6's per-icon visual `[ASSUMPTION]`. **`prds/time-slider/TRD.html` remains stale** against v2 *and* v3 — still no generator specified and still not mine to invent, flagged again for whoever owns it. `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` (T-036) sits modified and uncommitted in the shared tree from another session and was **not** swept into my commit — named on `BOARD.md` for `project-manager` per rule 2 / L-017 rather than only here.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `prds/time-slider/TRD.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry), `agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-032 readiness note), explicit paths, `git diff --staged` re-read hunk by hunk before committing — which changed what I committed twice. `chief-of-staff`'s founder-direct D6 icon-only edit was uncommitted in `TRD.md` while I was writing v3; that session committed it itself mid-pass (`passenger-brain 5220c1f`), so it is **not** carried in my commit and my earlier note saying it would be was corrected in both the TRD and this entry rather than left standing. **My `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` hunk does carry a second entry that is not mine** — `ios-code-reviewer`'s T-035/PAS-26 `trd-review` REQUEST CHANGES, left uncommitted in the shared tree by that session. It is self-signed, so nothing is misattributed, and committing it is better than leaving a finished verdict belonging to nobody (L-017); naming it here so the diff is not read as mine. Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated, rule 9.

### 2026-08-02 — ios-code-reviewer — T-035/PAS-26 `trd-review` (C1–C14 only): **REQUEST CHANGES** — install-id conflicts with `analytics/EVENTS.md`, req-9's "no type holds both" is false against the shipped `MapScreen`, and the VoiceOver-focus check §10 cites doesn't exist in §9

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass on T-035's C1–C14 `[iOS]` bulk, alongside `ios-developer`'s own pass (both signoffs required; A1/B1 out of scope, covered separately). Read `PROGRESS.md`'s `a6c1c73` architect entry in full, `BOARD.md`'s T-035 row, then `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md` and `tourist-trap-flag.md` in full. Per the dispatch's specific asks, checked three things against source rather than against the TRD's own prose: the install-id privacy reasoning (§3.3) for a correlation gap, whether req 9's "no type holds both `LocalQAAnswerStore` and either catalog" (§2.2) is actually how the module boundaries are drawn, and the toast's VoiceOver-focus behavior (D4). Cross-read `passenger-code/Passenger/Map/MapScreen.swift`, `Detail/HoodSheet.swift`, `Detail/PlaceDetailModal.swift`, `Places/SavedPlacesStore.swift` (confirms `SavedPlacesPersistence` actor shape §3.3 claims to mirror is real), and `passenger-brain/analytics/EVENTS.md`.
- **Finding 1 (blocking) — `InstallIdentity` (TRD §3.3, line 163) silently conflicts with `analytics/EVENTS.md`, which already names this TRD as the source of the identifier it reuses.** `EVENTS.md` (committed 2026-08-01, one day *before* this TRD, `2e38e4b`) states as a load-bearing "Identity constraint": *"Every table keys on an anonymous `install_id` — **the same identifier `local_qa_answers` already uses** (`prds/tourist-trap-flag/tourist-trap-flag.md`)"* (line 13), and defines that id as *"Client-generated, persisted in SwiftData"* (`app_installs.install_id`, line 22). This TRD's `InstallIdentity` is a different, independently-generated UUID that lives **only** inside `local-qa.json` (Application Support JSON, not SwiftData), deliberately walled inside `LocalQA/`, which §2.2 forbids from importing `Hoods/`/`Map/`/either catalog. The TRD never mentions `EVENTS.md`, `app_installs`, or `analytics-engineer` anywhere. Two independent generation paths will not produce the same UUID, so either: (a) `local_qa_answers.install_id` and `app_installs.install_id` silently diverge, breaking the exact analytics join `EVENTS.md` promises (can't correlate a local-QA answer to the rest of that install's funnel), discovered only when `analytics-engineer`/`developer` builds against both docs; or (b) whoever notices the mismatch "fixes" it by making `InstallIdentity` the same value as the app-wide analytics id — which would **defeat this TRD's own minimization design**, since `app_installs.install_id` is also the FK on every row of `app_events` (searches, screens, sessions — the full behavioral stream). Sharing it would let anyone with backend access join a person's entire in-app behavior to their local-QA visit history (place + hour), a materially bigger correlation surface than either table alone — the opposite of what §3.3's careful slug-only/hour-truncated/non-Keychain reasoning is trying to achieve. **Failure scenario:** `build` ships both features against their own docs as written, the two `install_id`s are silently different UUIDs, and the first person to notice is whoever tries to run `EVENTS.md`'s own funnel query joining local-QA behavior to retention — by which point it's a data-migration problem, not a TRD edit. **Fix required before `build`:** the TRD needs an explicit call (a new D9) on whether `InstallIdentity` is deliberately distinct from `app_installs.install_id` (and if so, say why unifying them would be worse, and flag `EVENTS.md`'s line 13 as needing correction) — or, if unification is truly intended, redesign `InstallIdentity`'s storage/ownership accordingly. Either answer is fine; leaving it unaddressed is not.
- **Finding 2 (blocking) — §2.2's "no type holds a reference to both `LocalQAAnswerStore` and either catalog" (line 78) is not how the module boundaries are actually drawn, checked against the shipped composition root.** `MapScreen.swift:58-62` already holds `placeCatalog` (`PlaceCatalog`, a catalog) as `@State`, alongside `hoods: [Hood]` loaded from `HoodCatalog`. Per §11 C11/C12, `LocalQACoordinator` (which per C9 will hold `LocalQAAnswerStore`) and `LocalQAPresenter` need an owner to wire the `VisitSource` stream and host the toast — and every existing precedent in this exact file (`densityStore`, `locationStore`, `savedPlacesStore`, `placeCatalog` are all `MapScreen`'s own `@State`) points at `MapScreen` doing that too. Once it does, `MapScreen` is a type holding a reference to both a catalog and — transitively, through `LocalQACoordinator` — `LocalQAAnswerStore`, which is exactly what §2.2 says no type may do, and exactly what §9 row 9(a) (line 378) tells `ios-code-reviewer` to give "a blocking review finding" for. As literally written, the TRD's own intended architecture trips its own review gate. **The invariant that actually holds** is narrower and is never stated as the load-bearing one: `FlagStroke.treatment(for:band:)` and `HoodSpeech.label(...)` (§4.1/§4.2) — the only two functions that decide what renders — never take `LocalQAAnswerStore` or any of its data as an input. That's the real, checkable guarantee behind req 9; "no type holds both" is not. **Fix required before `build`:** correct §2.2 and §9 row 9(a) to state and check the render-function-signature invariant (neither `FlagStroke.treatment` nor `HoodSpeech.label` may ever gain a `LocalQAAnswerStore`/answer parameter) rather than the broader claim the codebase's own existing shape already falsifies — otherwise `ios-code-reviewer`'s future pass either false-positives on `MapScreen` or quietly narrows the rule with no written record of having done so.
- **Finding 3 (blocking) — §10's risk table (line 393) claims "§9 row 8 has `qa` confirm focus is not stolen" for D4's new `UIWindow`; §9 row 8 (line 376) has no such check.** Read row 8's full observable/pass-condition columns: (a)–(e) cover `LocalQAGate` precedence, toast-appears-once, auto-dismiss timing, presenter-count, and queue state — nothing about `UIAccessibility`/VoiceOver focus anywhere in the row, nor in any other §9 row. This is the exact "looks right, no gate can fail" shape the TRD's own preamble (line 365, "No row's pass condition is 'looks right'") and L-018 exist to catch — landing on the one row governing D4's own stated highest-risk surface (a second `UIWindow` in an otherwise pure-SwiftUI app). Also worth naming while fixing this: D4 (line 345) only pins the **notification type** (`.announcement`, not `.screenChanged`) as the focus-preserving mechanism, but doesn't name the **window-presentation** mechanics that independently affect VoiceOver focus (whether the window becomes key via `makeKeyAndVisible`, `accessibilityViewIsModal`, window level relative to `.normal`) — a new `UIWindow` can pull VoiceOver focus into itself through UIKit's own hierarchy-change scanning regardless of which notification is explicitly posted. **Fix required before `build`:** add a real row-8(f) — or a dedicated row — with an observable (VoiceOver focus/`UIAccessibility.focusedElement` before vs. after the toast appears, checked via Accessibility Inspector or a UI test) and a falsifiable pass condition, and have C12's implementation notes name the window-presentation choices (key window or not, `accessibilityViewIsModal`) that keep focus from moving, not only the notification type.
- **Everything else checked and found sound:** D1–D8 all independently re-derived from source, not accepted on the TRD's word — `HoodSheet.swift` (read in full) confirms D5's premise exactly (no flag surface anywhere in the file); `PlaceDetailModal.swift:76-86` confirms `touristTrapSlot`'s reserved `EmptyView()` matches C7's target precisely; `SavedPlacesStore.swift`/`SavedPlacesPersistence` (actor, generation-guarded, Application-Support-JSON) confirms §3.3's "follows `SavedPlacesPersistence`'s actor shape exactly" claim is accurate, not aspirational. D3 (req 2/req 5 contradiction) and D5 (Hood-sheet surface) are correctly flagged to `product` rather than decided unilaterally — no objection to either from this seat. §9's per-P0 verification table is otherwise falsifiable, not "looks right," and the A1/T-032-seed cross-dependency (row 5) is named rather than hidden.
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES.** Three blocking findings, all cheap, spec-level fixes for `architect` (a new decision + two table/text corrections), none a redesign — same class of fix T-032's `trd-review` round needed. Findings 1 and 3 are both instances of the same shape: a claim made confidently in one place (§10's risk table, `EVENTS.md`'s identity constraint) that a careful second read shows isn't backed by what's actually specified or built elsewhere. Finding 2 is the one this dispatch specifically asked to verify rather than accept, and it doesn't hold as literally stated once checked against the shipped `MapScreen.swift`.
- **Left behind:** the three findings above, for `architect` to fix and resubmit; `ios-code-reviewer` re-reviews the fix, not the whole TRD again. Finding 1 also needs a decision from whoever owns `analytics/EVENTS.md` (`analytics-engineer`) once `architect` states which way T-035 is resolving it. Not touching `BOARD.md`'s state — that's `chief-of-staff`'s per this task's own routing. `ios-developer`'s own independent `trd-review` signoff is still needed; if it lands at APPROVE, the two verdicts disagree and this task should route back to `architect` regardless, per the same precedent T-032 set.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry), explicit path. **This file's diff unavoidably also carries a concurrent architect session's T-032/PAS-15 v3 worklog entry ("all four `trd-review` findings resolved"), already sitting uncommitted immediately above the insertion point when this pass opened the file** — not this pass's writing, adjacent lines, same hunk-merging limitation this exact file's own T-035 architect entry documents above (`git diff` cannot hunk-filter adjacent additions). Re-read the full diff before committing per rule 2 and confirmed that entry's content is untouched, not authored, edited, or removed by this pass — called out here rather than left to read as mine, same disclosure precedent that other session's own commit used minutes earlier in this file for an analogous adjacent-hunk conflict. No `passenger-code` changes (review-only pass). Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated, rule 9.

### 2026-08-02 — code-reviewer — T-035/PAS-26 `trd-review` (A1 only): **APPROVE WITH MINORS** — round-trip mechanism verified sound, but the TRD's "byte-identical" claim is false for the JSON bundle

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass on T-035's step A1 (fake Hood tourist-trap flag values, `[Algo/Data]`), alongside `data-engineer`'s own pass — both signoffs required. Read `PROGRESS.md`'s `a6c1c73` architect entry in full and `BOARD.md`'s T-035 rows, then verified the A1 plan against the real generator rather than trusting the TRD's or the architect's description of it, per the task brief (this is exactly the kind of claim L-024 says needs a skeptical second read).
- **Evidence — round-trip mechanism is real, not just claimed:** ran `database/scripts/build_hoods.py --migration-number 006` against the **unmodified** committed source to a scratch path. The regenerated `migrations/006_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql` diffed **byte-identical** to the committed file (confirmed: TRD's claim holds for the SQL artifact). Then edited a scratch copy of the source to flip two Hoods' `isTouristTrap` (`null→true`, `null→false`) and regenerated again: both the JSON bundle and the SQL migration changed **only** the two touched hoods' flag value/column — every polygon, blurb, and unrelated row was byte-identical in both artifacts. This is the actual anti-drift property A1 depends on, and it holds.
- **Finding 1 (blocking-for-precision, not for A1 itself) — the TRD's "byte-identical" round-trip claim is false for the JSON bundle, specifically.** `build_bundle()` in `build_hoods.py` stamps `"generatedAt": datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)...` — wall-clock time, not derived from source content. I regenerated from the **unmodified** committed source and diffed against the shipped `passenger-code/Passenger/Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json`: the only difference was `generatedAt` (`2026-08-02T17:31:42Z` vs the shipped `2026-07-31T22:44:41Z`) — i.e. the bundle is **never** byte-identical across two runs, even with zero content change. The SQL migration is unaffected (no timestamp field, only a stable source-digest comment) — that half of the claim is genuinely true. **Failure scenario:** the TRD (line ~389) and its own docstring describe the round-trip check as "byte-identical... the exact check that caught L-024." If `qa` builds `TEST-PLAN.md` off that literal description and diffs the full JSON file, the check fails on every run regardless of correctness — which either produces false alarms or, more dangerously, trains whoever runs it to expect "some diff" and eyeball past it, which is precisely the failure mode L-024 already burned this project on (a real polygon/geometry drift could hide in that same "expected" noise). **Fix:** the TRD's round-trip description and `qa`'s derived check need to exclude/normalize `generatedAt` explicitly, or `build_hoods.py` should derive it from the source digest instead of wall-clock time.
- **Finding 2 (minor) — the authoring rule's 5 conditions are not tool-enforced.** `database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json`'s authoring rule (≥1 busy+flagged-in-T-032's-seed, ≥1 flagged-never-busy, ≥1 explicit `false`, ≥1 `null`, ≤4 flagged of 24) has no corresponding check in `validate_dataset.py` — `V9` only validates that `isTouristTrap` is `bool`/`null`-typed, nothing checks the distribution or count. A source edit violating the rule (e.g. 10 hoods flagged, or missing the explicit-`false` case) passes `--check` and the generator writes both artifacts without complaint. Not a defect in A1's design — it's inherent to a prose authoring rule — but flagged so the **actual** A1 review (once `data-engineer` submits the real edited source) explicitly counts against the five conditions rather than trusting a clean `build_hoods.py` run, which proves nothing about the distribution.
- **Confirmed, not a new finding — migration `006` is genuinely still unapplied.** `database/README.md`'s status table: `006` is "Not applied — blocked-on-aviran" and depends on `003` (adds the `is_tourist_trap` column), which is also unapplied. Regenerating `006` now is safe — nothing in a live DB depends on its current state, because there is no live DB state yet.
- **Confirmed, not a new finding — criterion 1 of the authoring rule is currently unverifiable.** Searched the repo for any T-032 density-seed artifact (`density-seed-tel-aviv.json` or similar): none exists. T-032's C10 has not landed, matching what the TRD and board row already state plainly. Not hidden, so not blocking, but restating for the record: if `data-engineer` executes A1 before T-032's seed lands, their pick for "the busy+flagged Hood" is provisional and needs a real second look once the seed exists, not a rubber-stamp reconciliation.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS, for A1.** The core design is correct and matches L-024's lesson exactly — edits go through the source, never the bundle, and the generator's surgical-change property is real (verified directly, not assumed). Findings 1 and 2 are both precision/tooling gaps in the verification story around A1, not defects in A1's plan itself, and neither blocks `data-engineer` from proceeding. Finding 1 should be fixed before `qa` writes `TEST-PLAN.md`'s round-trip check off the TRD's current wording.
- **Left behind:** Finding 1 (generatedAt breaks the literal byte-identical claim — needs a TRD wording fix or a generator fix, whichever `data-engineer`/`architect` prefer) and Finding 2 (authoring rule uncounted by tooling — reviewer must hand-count on the real A1 diff) for whoever does the follow-up pass once `data-engineer` submits actual seeded values. No file changes made this session — verification only, via scratch copies under `/tmp`, nothing touched in the repo.
- **Git:** no changes to commit — this was a verification-only pass against already-committed files.

### 2026-08-02 — ios-code-reviewer — T-034/PAS-25 `trd-review`: **APPROVE WITH MINORS**

- **Did:** independent `trd-review` pass on `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md` (v1, `7c4490f`) against the PRD (`live-events-overlay.md`, Draft v2), `map-rendering-spec.md`, `design-principles.md` §2/§3/§5, and shipped source — `MapScreen.swift`, `DetailRouter.swift`, `BuildPhase.swift`, `PlaceLayer.swift` — rather than trusting the TRD's own citations of that code.
- **D1 and D4 correctly scoped, confirmed by re-deriving rather than trusting the TRD's framing.** D1 (hour binding widened from start-hour to overlap) is a genuine reading-of-the-PRD question and correctly routed to `product`, plus a separate, narrower confirmation to `data-engineer` on the query-string amendment only — the split is right: `product` owns whether the requirement means what the TRD reads it to mean, `data-engineer` owns whether dropping T-043 §4.2's `start_at=gte` filter is safe against the approved view (it is — `events_public` already enforces `end_at > now()`, verified by reading `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §4.2's cited filter list, not assumed). D4 (clustering deferred to unowned T-041) follows L-018 correctly: no invented pass condition, §9 states plainly it isn't verifiable, and the recommendation is stated rather than silently acted on.
- **Zero-new-backend-calls-in-Phase-1 claim verified, not assumed.** `Support/BuildPhase.swift` today holds exactly one runtime constant (`seedIsAuthoritative = true`) driving `PlaceCatalog`'s seed-only path with no fetch attempted; the TRD's `eventSeedIsAuthoritative` is the identical mechanism applied to a second store. `MapScreen.swift`'s four existing `.task` blocks confirm the seed-vs-fetch branch pattern is real, shipped, and already reviewed (T-032's `trd-review`) — the TRD is reusing a proven seam, not proposing an unverified one.
- **D2's "made safe by the cap" claim is only partly supported — one real gap, not blocking.** The TRD rebuts the pin-density reasoning (`map-rendering-spec.md` §2's pin row is about "dozens of place pins," not a 12-marker cap) but never engages the *other*, more directly applicable principle in the same doc: §3's explicit commitment that city-wide zoom stays "a pure density read... not a liability map at every scale," argued on **legibility grounds independent of count** ("a map you have to parse for what's the exception, not a glance"). Twelve markers layered over heat blobs at the exact zoom a cold open shows is arguably the same "map you have to parse" problem Aviran's original tag pushback described, regardless of the count being bounded. The TRD already flags D2 for `designer`/`product` — correct process — but whoever adjudicates it should know the written justification answers the wrong precedent, not that the divergence is unreviewed.
- **New finding, not raised by the TRD: `DetailRouter.openEvent` is asymmetric with `openPlace`'s actual shipped behavior, and the stated reason doesn't hold.** §4.7 justifies always-depth-1/always-exclusive event routing by comparing it to `openHood`'s swap-in-place behavior and rejecting a depth-2 alternative as one that "would add a third level" the router "deliberately cannot express." That's not accurate: depth 2 already exists (`hood != nil && place != nil`), and an event could occupy that same slot the way `place` does when a Hood sheet is open — `openPlace`'s own shipped comment (`DetailRouter.swift:20-22`) states this exact case is intentional ("A pin tap on the exposed map while the Hood sheet is open lands at depth 2 — correct, the user is still inside that Hood's context"). As specified, tapping a visible event marker while a Hood sheet is open (reachable today — `MapScreen.swift:186`'s `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))` keeps the map tappable up through medium detent) would force-close the Hood sheet the user is browsing, where an equivalent tap on a place pin in the same situation nests instead and preserves it. No PRD requirement addresses this case either way, and the TRD presents it as settled rather than flagging it the way D1/D2/D4/D6 are flagged. Recommend `product`/`designer` get an explicit call here too, or `ios-developer` builds event as a `DetailRouter` case parallel to `place`'s depth-2 behavior instead.
- **Accessibility gap, minor.** §4.5's VoiceOver label (`"\(event.name), event, \(timeLabel)"`) leaves `timeLabel`'s format undefined, and §9 has no row asserting the label's actual content — only the tap-target identifier (`eventMarker-<id>`) is tested. `map-rendering-spec.md` §7 holds every other map annotation (zones, pins, clusters, personal-place pins) to an exact quoted string; this is the one new annotation type in the app without one. Not a missing label (design-principles.md §5's bar), so not a blocking accessibility failure — but worth a one-line format spec and a unit test before C5/C8, the same rigor `EventDetailRows` gets for the sheet body.
- **D7 (tap-race fix) confirmed real, not asserted.** Read `MapScreen.swift:113-122` directly: the greedy `SpatialTapGesture` via `.simultaneousGesture` is exactly as described, and `handleTap`'s current place-then-hood precedence would resolve an event-marker tap to the wrong sheet without the TRD's fix. The mirrored `PlaceHitTester`/idempotent-`openPlace` pattern this fix copies is real and already shipping.
- **No accessibility failure and no functional/reliability break found** (per the Maslow precedence this role applies) — everything above is a specification gap or an unflagged-but-reversible design call, not a broken or unsafe build. Verdict: **APPROVE WITH MINORS.**

- **D7 checked concretely against `ux-flows.md` §2 — accurate, well-sourced, not overstated.** §2's Primary table (line 46) reads verbatim: *"Saved-places icon (4th icon) — Persistent icon, **separate from the 3 nav buttons**; opens the full Places list."* §2.1's 2026-07-30 stacking addendum (line 85) independently lists *"near-me (L44) and the Places icon (L46)"* together by name as bucket-2 chrome that fades under an open modal — the exact bucket T-036 §2.4/§4.5 places `PlacesButton` in. Both citations check out word-for-word against the live doc, not paraphrased.
- **This is a real conflict with T-032's TRD, and it's in the prose, not the type contract.** T-032 §8 D1 (`prds/time-slider/TRD.md:499-500`) reads: *"`ux-flows.md` §2 and the mockup show three or four nav buttons... This task builds `MapNavRow` as the container plus the heat button only. **The row is laid out so the rest slot in without re-layout.**"* — naming Search/Profile/Places in the line just above as "the rest." That's a textual claim that Places will physically join `MapNavRow`, which is what T-036's D7 corrects (Places is bucket-2 chrome, never the nav row). T-036 §2.4/§8 D7 says as much ("This corrects T-032's D1's expectation... for T-036 it is not") but the correction lives only in T-036's document — T-032's own D1 text still asserts the stale expectation as written today. Distinct from `ios-developer`'s finding below: that one is a type-shape bug in a Swift contract; this one is a stale claim in prose that a future reader (an architect scoping T-038, a designer reading T-032 cold) would reasonably take at face value. **Recommend folding a one-line correction into T-032's D1** ("the rest" → "Search and Profile; Places is bucket-2 chrome, corrected by T-036's D7") **into the v3 pass already in flight** for T-032 (`BOARD.md` line 54) rather than opening a fourth pass later — same document, same trip. Non-blocking for T-036 itself, whose own text is internally consistent and correctly sourced.
- **Blocking — independently re-verified `ios-developer`'s `NavSurface` type-mismatch finding, concur.** T-036 §4.5 (`TRD.md:304`) specs `enum NavSurface: Equatable { case heat, places }` with the comment "whichever task lands first declares its own case." Read T-032's real, current §4.1 (`prds/time-slider/TRD.md:188-191`) directly: `enum NavSurface: String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable { case search, heat, places, profile }`, built **unconditionally** at T-032's C1, with its own commentary (`:205`) stating the four-member set exists specifically "to stop T-036/T-037/T-038 each inventing a private boolean" — i.e., T-032 already considers this file settled and non-negotiable, the opposite of "whoever lands first declares their own case." Different case count (2 vs 4), different conformances (`Equatable` alone vs `String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable`). T-036's own build step C6 (`:487`) — "build only if T-032's C1 has not landed" — would create the narrower 2-case stub in the live ordering risk the dispatch names (T-036 lands before T-032's v3 clears), and T-032's C1 is unconditional with no "if it exists, don't restructure" clause anywhere in its own text. That is exactly the restructuring T-036 §2.2 promises won't happen, promised unilaterally. **Fix, same as `ios-developer` recommended:** T-036 §4.5/§2.2/C6 should declare the actual four-case T-032 contract verbatim and make C6's conditional "if it doesn't exist, create it exactly per T-032 §4.1," not a narrower invented type.
- **Precedence-merge logic (`PlaceProvenance: Comparable`, §4.1/§4.3) — sound. No path to legitimate ambiguity found.** Precedence is `max` over an `Int`-backed `Comparable` enum (`visited=0, been=1, saved=2`); a place's provenance is never stored, only computed at read time from two independent sources (`saved: Set<Place.ID>`, `visits: [Place.ID: VisitKind]`). `VisitKind` is a **separate** type from `PlaceProvenance` with no `.saved` case, so the visit source cannot produce the top value by construction — there is no branch that could resolve it backwards. The one place ambiguity could theoretically enter — two visit records for the same `place_id` — is closed at the boundary: §4.2 requires "keep the higher kind, drop the other" on a duplicate id, enforced by a test (C4), so `[Place.ID: VisitKind]` can hold at most one value per key by the time `PlacesListComposition.entries` ever sees it. No third store, no persisted merged state, nothing to go stale. **One should-fix, non-blocking:** §3.4's "constraint the real detector inherits" list states the record shape (`{place_id, kind, first_observed_at}`) but not the **update** invariant — keep-the-higher-kind-on-revisit — that currently exists only as a Phase-1 fixture-authoring rule (§4.2, tested by C4). PRD req 3 bullet 4 ("revisiting a Been place... changes no label") depends on that same invariant surviving into the real detector's write path in Phase 2, and §3.4 is exactly where the TRD already writes down what the detector must inherit — recommend adding one line there so the invariant isn't re-discovered (or silently violated) when the fixture is swapped out.
- **Privacy / no-new-backend-surface claim — confirmed, checked three ways independently rather than accepted from §1.** (1) `VisitedPlacesStore` (§4.2) has no persistence and no `CoreLocation` import anywhere in its contract — read-only fixture in, in-memory dictionary out, matches §3.4's claim. (2) The one server-side field this feature reads, `places.permanently_closed`, is pre-existing scope already owned and approved by T-042's TRD (§3 schema, migration `004`, held for Build Phase 2) — this task adds no column, no table, no RLS, no endpoint; it only widens an existing `select=` string by one column. (3) `saved-places.json` is untouched (D4's own reasoning depends on this) and the Been/Visited fixture is bundle-read-only, never written. §9 row 5 makes the "no `CoreLocation` import, no authorization read" claim a grep-checkable review item rather than a promise — correct instinct, and it holds on inspection.
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES**, net result unchanged by concurring rather than disagreeing — both signoffs required and the `NavSurface` mismatch is a real, independently-confirmed blocking bug regardless of which reviewer names it first. Two items for the fix pass: (1) blocking — T-036 §4.5/§2.2/C6 must adopt T-032's real four-case `NavSurface` contract verbatim; (2) should-fix, non-blocking — fold a one-line D1 correction into T-032's own v3 pass so its prose stops asserting Places will join `MapNavRow`. Everything else — D2, D3, D4–D6, D8, D9, the §9 table, the build breakdown — checked out on independent re-derivation and is not re-litigated here.
- **Evidence:** direct reads of `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` (full), `places-been-saved.md` (full), `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` §4.1/§8 D1 (full sections), `design/ux-flows.md` §2/§2.1 (full sections, both citations verified verbatim), `agent-os/BOARD.md` T-032/T-036 rows.

### 2026-08-02 — ios-developer — T-036/PAS-27 `trd-review`: **REQUEST CHANGES** — the shared `MapChromeState` contract is not actually shared

- **Did:** read `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` (v1) in full against the PRD (`places-been-saved.md`, Draft v1), `BOARD.md`'s T-036 row, and the relevant `PROGRESS.md` history (architect's 2026-08-02 T-036 TRD entry, `0051ef0`). - **D1 verified accurate.** `Place` today is exactly the 5-field shape the TRD says (`id, name, category, hoodID, coordinate`), no `permanentlyClosed`. All three of `PlaceCatalog`'s source paths currently and deliberately exclude the field: `SeedFile.Entry`'s own comment says so explicitly ("`permanently_closed`... deliberately undeclared"), `PlacesAPI.PlaceRow` + its `select=` query string carry no such column, and `PlacesCache.CachedPlace` carries no such field either. Threading it through all three, non-optional, is real work and not already partially done — the TRD's claim checks out.
- **D8 verified accurate.** `DetailRouter.swift`'s `closeHood()`/`closePlace()`/`openPlace(_:)`/`placeDepth` are exactly as described, and `MapScreen.swift:186` does carry `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))` — the mechanism §4.6 builds its exclusivity rule on is real, not asserted.
- **Blocking finding — §2.2's "identical contract, either build order" claim is false, checked against T-032's actual TRD text, not assumed.** T-036 §4.5 specs:
  ```swift
  enum NavSurface: Equatable { case heat, places }   // §2.2: whichever task lands first declares its own case
  ```
  T-032's real, current §4.1 (v2, the version live right now — its pending v3 fix is scoped to an edge-gesture camera-immobility test, nowhere near this section) specs a **different type**:
  ```swift
  enum NavSurface: String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable {
      case search, heat, places, profile
      var id: String { rawValue }
  }
  ```
  Different case membership (2 vs. 4), different protocol conformances (`Equatable` only vs. `String, CaseIterable, Sendable, Identifiable`), and — the part that actually breaks the "either build order" claim — **no reciprocal coordination logic on T-032's side**. T-032's TRD text (§4.1's own commentary, plus §7's dependency-direction line: *"T-036/T-037/T-038 add their own `NavSurface` views and their own buttons to `MapNavRow`"*) states flatly that it ships the full four-member set up front because `ux-flows.md` §2.1 locks it, specifically to stop T-036/T-037/T-038 each inventing a private type. T-032's own build step (C1, unconditional, no "if it already exists" clause) just builds that file. There is no "whoever builds it second doesn't restructure it" language anywhere in T-032's TRD — that promise exists only in T-036's document, unilaterally, describing an agreement the other party never made.
  - **Concretely, in the order the dispatch flagged as live right now (T-036 lands before T-032's v3 clears review):** T-036's own build step C6 says "build `MapChromeState`+`NavSurface.places` only if T-032's C1 has not landed" and would create the 2-case `Equatable` stub per §4.5. When T-032 later builds its unconditional C1 against a file that already exists with the wrong shape, it has to change the case count (2→4), add three conformances, and rename the raw representation — that is restructuring, not "add one case," directly violating T-036's own §2.2 guarantee to whoever builds second.
  - **In the reverse order (T-032 lands first)** there's no collision, because T-032's real enum is a superset that already contains `.places` — T-036 would just read the shipped file and reuse it. But that's exactly why the fix is cheap: T-036 §4.5 should be corrected to declare the *actual* T-032 §4.1 contract verbatim (all four cases, real conformances) rather than a narrower invented one, and its build-step C6 conditional should read "if it doesn't exist yet, create it exactly per T-032 §4.1" — not a 2-case subset. That makes both build orders safe, including the live one.
- **Everything else checked out on this pass — D2–D7, D9, the §9 verification table, and the build breakdown are sound**, and the two "carried, not claimed" honesty notes (§9 rows 3/5 on the Been/Visited fixture-vs-detector gap, row 4 on T-044's ownerless freshness job) are accurate reads of what's actually built vs. what's asserted. Not re-litigating those; only the MapChromeState mismatch blocks.

### 2026-08-02 — ios-developer — T-034/PAS-25 `trd-review`: APPROVE WITH MINORS — one real mutual-exclusivity gap found in §4.7, everything else checked out against shipped code

- **Did:** read `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md` against the PRD (Draft v2) and against the actual shipped Swift it claims to build on — `Map/MapScreen.swift`, `Map/PlaceLayer.swift`, `Places/PlaceHitTester.swift`, `Detail/DetailRouter.swift`, `Support/BuildPhase.swift` — and against `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §4.2 for the backend-surface claim. No code written; this is a pre-build review pass.
- **D7 (tap precedence) verified concretely, not taken on the TRD's word.** `MapScreen.swift:113-122`'s `.simultaneousGesture(SpatialTapGesture()...)` is exactly as cited — greedy, fires on every tap alongside `PlaceLayer`'s own `Button`. Read `handleTap` (267-278): it's an if/else-if chain, place-then-hood, both branches gated identically to what they render (`showsNames`). The TRD's fix — `event (ungated) → place (gated) → hood`, `EventHitTester` reusing the same 22-screen-point `mapPointTolerance` — slots into that exact shape. For the actual race the PRD/TRD name (tap an event marker → `Button` fires `openEvent` directly, `SpatialTapGesture` fires `handleTap`), the event branch matches first and calls `openEvent` again — idempotent, same pattern `PlaceLayer`'s own doc comment states and `DetailRouter.openPlace` already proves out. **This specific race is correctly fixed as specified.**
- **The "idempotent two-path pattern" D7 says it mirrors does exist as described.** `PlaceLayer.swift`'s header comment states it verbatim ("both this button and the map's own `SpatialTapGesture` can fire... both call `router.openPlace`... a safe no-op the second time"), and `DetailRouter.openPlace` is in fact just `self.place = place` — idempotent by construction. Not asserted from the TRD's citation; read directly.
- **Real finding, not raised by the TRD: §4.7's mutual-exclusivity claim doesn't hold in both directions.** The TRD states the event destination is "always depth 1, and mutually exclusive with both other destinations," and its code sample makes `openEvent` clear `hood`/`place` — but the *existing*, unmodified `openHood` (`hood = hood; place = nil`) and `openPlace` (`place = place`) are never told to clear `event`, and the TRD's §4.7 diff doesn't add that either (only `closeHood` is named as needing the update, for the swipe-to-dismiss path). This is reachable, not theoretical: the event sheet ships with `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))` — the same mechanism T-033's shipped sheets already rely on to let taps reach the map underneath a `.medium`-detent sheet. So: open an event sheet, then tap the exposed map (a Hood, or a place pin, still visible above the sheet) → `openHood`/`openPlace` fires and sets `hood`/`place`, but `event` stays non-nil. If the sheet's `Group` checks `event` first (the natural order, matching D7's event-first precedence), the sheet silently keeps showing the just-tapped-away-from event instead of switching — a stale sheet bug, not a crash, but a real one, and exactly the class of gate-mismatch this codebase has already shipped a REJECT for once (T-033's zoom/hit-test gate mismatch, cited in the TRD's own §4.5). Mechanical fix: `openHood` and `openPlace` each need `event = nil` alongside their existing clears, same as `openEvent` already clears both. §9 row 4 should gain a case (open an event, tap a Hood/place, confirm the sheet switches) alongside its existing D7 check.
- **§9 verification table:** every P0 row has a falsifiable observable and pass condition with an independent computation (not read back from the function under test) — confirmed by reading all six rows, not sampling. Row 2c (clustering) is the one deliberate exception, explicitly marked "no pass condition — not verifiable in this task" with the reasoning stated (L-018 discipline) rather than a "looks right" row faking coverage. Correct, not a gap.
- **Zero-backend-surface claim checked independently, not taken on the TRD's citation.** Read `live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §4.2 directly: the `events_public` view's `select` list (`id, name, start_at, end_at, lat, lng, venue_name, hood_id, category, rank, source_name`) and `where` clause (`withdrawn_at is null and hood_id is not null and end_at > now() and rank >= event_rank_floor()`) match T-034's TRD verbatim. The one contract edit — dropping the client's `start_at=gte.{anchorHour}` lower bound — is a query-string change in a request `EventsAPI` builds (C7); no view/SQL/RLS edit falls out of it, confirmed by reading the view's own filter, which already enforces `end_at > now()` regardless of what the client's lower bound was doing. All twelve C1–C12 steps read `[iOS]`; none touches `database/`. T-043 (the pipeline) independently confirmed `build`-ready, 4/4 `trd-review` (`BOARD.md` line 65/70) — the contract T-034 builds against is settled, not provisional.
- **C12's T-032 dependency confirmed real, correctly isolated.** `HeatModalCard.swift` doesn't exist in `passenger-code` yet (grep confirms) — T-032 is mid-`trd-review` (SPLIT, back to `trd` for a v3 fix, `BOARD.md` line 54), so the toggle step genuinely has no file to land in yet. The TRD already scopes this to C12 alone and says build C1–C11 regardless; that scoping is accurate against the current state of both tasks.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS.** The architecture, D7's precedence fix, the zero-backend-surface claim, and the §9 table are all sound and buildable as written. One concrete, mechanical fix needed before/during C9: `openHood` and `openPlace` must also clear `event`, matching `openEvent`'s existing clear of both. Not a redesign, not a blocker on C1–C8/C10/C11.
- **Evidence:** direct reads of `Passenger/Map/MapScreen.swift`, `Passenger/Map/PlaceLayer.swift`, `Passenger/Places/PlaceHitTester.swift`, `Passenger/Detail/DetailRouter.swift`, `Passenger/Support/BuildPhase.swift`, `prds/live-events-pipeline/TRD.md` §4.2, `agent-os/BOARD.md` T-032/T-034/T-043 rows; `find` confirming `HeatModalCard.swift` absent from `passenger-code`.
- **Left behind:** the `openHood`/`openPlace` clearing-`event` fix, for whoever builds C9 (or the architect, if `product`/`chief-of-staff` want it folded into the TRD text first — it's small enough to fix at build time without a v2). `data-engineer`'s targeted confirmation on §4.2's amendment and `product`'s four flagged calls (D1, D4, D10, D2) are still outstanding, unchanged by this review — not this agent's to resolve. `BOARD.md` state not touched, per brief.

### 2026-08-02 — architect — T-035/PAS-26 TRD delivered: Tourist-trap flag & Local QA — not iOS-only, two PRD requirements found unsatisfiable as written

- **Did:** wrote `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md` (v1, ready for `trd-review`) from the approved PRD (Draft v1) plus the retired-gate design spec as reference. Eight decisions D1–D8, a §9 verification table with one row per P0 (plus a separate row for the leg Phase 1 cannot verify), and a 17-step build breakdown: **A1 `[Algo/Data]`, B1 `[Backend]` (HELD, Build Phase 2), C1–C14 `[iOS]`**. `trd-review` needs **three pairs** — `ios-developer` + `ios-code-reviewer`, `data-engineer` + `code-reviewer` (A1), `developer` + `code-reviewer` (B1) — plus `product` on three flagged calls.
- **Surface determined, not assumed — and it is not iOS-only.** The design spec's own §8 item 3 says "the fixture needs 2–3 Hoods seeded with real values," which reads as an iOS edit and would have walked `ios-developer` straight into **L-024**: `Passenger/Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` is a *generated* artifact — `database/scripts/build_hoods.py` emits it **and** `006_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql` from `database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json` in one run (read the generator, not the docs describing it). So Hood flag seeding is **A1, `[Algo/Data]`, in `passenger-brain`**: edit the source, regenerate, migration stays unapplied. `places-tel-aviv.json` is genuinely different and *is* hand-editable in Phase 1 — `hood-place-detail/TRD.md` C3a hand-authored it and T-042's `export_places.py` only becomes sole emitter at Phase 2 — so place flags are `[iOS]` C6. Two files that look identical, opposite rules; both checked against their own TRDs.
- **Finding 1 (D3): PRD reqs 2 and 5 contradict each other, and neither is checkable until it's resolved.** Req 2 bullet 3: *"Moving the time slider repaints heat and changes no stroke: the flag is not time-variant."* Req 5: a flagged Hood **above the busy threshold for the selected hour** gets a distinct stroke. Busy is per-hour, so a flagged Hood's stroke does change with the slider. Built the reconciled reading — for every hour 0…12 the **set** of Hoods rendering any flag stroke is identical; only an already-flagged Hood's stroke *style* varies — and wrote that as §9 row 2's falsifiable pass condition. **Flagged for `product`, not decided unilaterally:** if the intent was a fully hour-invariant stroke, req 5 is the requirement that changes.
- **Finding 2 (D5): req 4 bullet 2 has no surface anywhere in the app.** It says a not-yet-rated Hood "renders identically to not-flagged, **resolved on tap by the Hood sheet**." Read `HoodSheet.swift` directly: it has no flag surface of any kind; the PRD's req 6 covers the *place* modal only; the design spec's §0 explicitly scopes the Hood sheet out. As it stood, the tap resolves nothing and the bullet is prose no gate could fail — L-018's exact shape. Added a three-state line (flagged / not flagged / no local rating yet), flagged to `product`/`designer` as new UI no doc covers, with the "Not a tourist-heavy spot" phrasing named as an unapproved copy decision. One `@ViewBuilder` property; trivially reversible.
- **Finding 3: the local-QA loop's trigger does not exist and is not this task's to build.** No notification code, no region monitoring, no `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription`, no background mode anywhere in the project; `LocationStore` deliberately holds authorization status and never a `CLLocation`. The PRD assigns that detector to `data-engineer` as *"one detector, three consumers"* (T-035/T-036/T-037) and T-036's design flagged the same absence independently — **it is named in three PRDs and owned in none.** Phase 1 therefore builds the consumer side for real (toast, gate, ask-once ledger, device-local queue, install identity) against a `VisitSource` seam driven by a launch argument, the same construction the shipped `-uiTestZoomedIn` uses. §1 and §9 state plainly that Phase-1 acceptance does **not** cover req 8's first bullet. Recommended as its own board row, with its unowned Always-authorization / background-mode / notification-permission surface (and no PRD-specified moment to ask, since the strategy forbids onboarding).
- **Finding 4 (D4): the design's toast construction doesn't work.** It specifies a root `ZStack` overlay rendering over an open sheet; SwiftUI presents `.sheet` in its own host above the presenting view's overlays, and the notification-tap path foregrounds the app to whatever it was last showing — which can be a sheet. Call: a passthrough `UIWindow` whose `hitTest` returns `nil` outside the toast, so req 8's non-blocking bullet is structural rather than a styling choice. Cost stated: one UIKit bridge in an otherwise pure-SwiftUI app, and `qa` must confirm the second window doesn't steal VoiceOver focus (`.announcement`, never `.screenChanged`).
- **Also corrected against source:** the design spec says the flag *stroke* can reuse the existing `showsNames` threshold. It can't — `nameLabelSpanThreshold` (0.06) is the **Close** boundary (it gates pins and tap resolution too), so a stroke gated on it would appear exactly when pins do and the Neighborhood tier of `map-rendering-spec.md` §2's table would not exist. Both stroke and label need the new coarser threshold; they differ in which side of the Close boundary they survive. `MapZoomTier` is one pure derivation and `showsNames` becomes `tier == .close`, so four consumers can't disagree.
- **Cross-task dependency, named not worked around:** req 5's busy+flagged state is **unobservable until T-032's density seed (its C10) lands** — `DensityStore` has no seed path, so `band` is `nil` for every Hood at every hour in Phase 1. A second density seed in this task was rejected (two seeds is two chances to disagree); A1's authoring rule instead requires its flagged Hood ids to be reconciled with T-032's seed by whichever lands second.
- **Privacy treated as a data-model constraint, per the role's own rule:** `local-qa.json` records that a person was *physically present* at a named place, which is a stronger artifact than `saved-places.json`'s record of intent. Slug only (never a coordinate), timestamp truncated to the UTC hour, excluded from device backup, `.completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication` file protection, and a per-install `UUID` that is **not** the IDFV, not the IDFA and deliberately **not** Keychain-backed (the Keychain survives reinstall, which would make "install id" a lie and create a more durable identifier than the feature needs). Flagged as a possible sibling finding that `saved-places.json` gets none of this — deliberately not changed here (surgical-changes rule).
- **Req 9 made structural rather than conventional:** there is exactly one direction of data flow (catalogs → render, toast → answer store) and **no type holds both `LocalQAAnswerStore` and either catalog**, so "one answer never changes what any viewer sees" is a fact about the reference graph, not a behaviour to test. `ios-code-reviewer` gets a blocking finding for any type that acquires both. Same posture for req 5: `FlagStroke` is a single-value enum, so "replaces, never stacks" is unrepresentable rather than merely tested.
- **Evidence:** every claim checked against source — `HoodLayer.swift`, `HoodCatalog.swift`, `Hood.swift`, `Place.swift`, `PlaceCatalog.swift`, `PlacesAPI.swift`, `PlacesCache.swift`, `PlaceDetailModal.swift`, `HoodSheet.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`, `SavedPlacesStore.swift`, `LocationStore.swift`, `BuildPhase.swift`, `HeatBand.swift`, `project.pbxproj`'s Info.plist keys, the Assets catalog, `build_hoods.py`, `validate_dataset.py`, and both bundled fixtures (confirmed directly: **all 24 Hoods and all 9 places ship `null` today**). Migration number `007` assigned per the house rule that the number is passed in, never scanned (003/004/005/006 all claimed).
- **Left behind:** three `product` calls (D3's requirement contradiction, D5's new Hood-sheet surface, §1's Phase-1 boundary); the unowned dwell-detector row; `map-rendering-spec.md` §7's decision-#42-violating example ("not a tourist trap") still uncorrected — now flagged twice, by the design spec and by this TRD, and still owned by nobody. B1 must not be dispatched in Phase 1. `qa` builds `TEST-PLAN.md` from §9 and must treat the "8-trigger" row as explicitly out of Phase-1 scope rather than passing it by construction.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` **`a671373`** — `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md` (new), `agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-035 rows ×2), `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated, rule 9.
- **Self-report — I clobbered another session's commit and then undid it; saying so rather than leaving the reflog to say it (rule 2 / L-023, a new variant worth recording).** Three sessions wrote `BOARD.md` and `PROGRESS.md` in this working tree during this run. Rule 2's `git diff --staged` check *worked* — it caught a T-034 row that wasn't mine — but the fix I reached for was wrong: `git diff` merged T-034's row and my T-035 row into **one hunk** (adjacent lines), so hunk-level filtering could not separate them, and I moved to a private `GIT_INDEX_FILE` seeded with `git read-tree HEAD`. Between that `read-tree` and the commit, the concurrent session committed T-034's TRD — so my tree, built from the older HEAD, **reverted it**: commit `edb5c81` deleted `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md` (503 lines) and reverted both shared files' T-034 content. Caught immediately in my own `git show --stat`, undone with `git reset --soft 7c4490f` (never pushed, nothing lost — `prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md`, `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md`, T-034's PROGRESS entry and both its BOARD rows all verified present afterwards), and recommitted correctly on top of `7c4490f`. **The transferable lesson, for `retrospective`:** rule 2 protects against staging someone else's *content*; it says nothing about a stale *base*, and a private index seeded from a HEAD that then moves is a worse footgun than the shared index it was meant to avoid. When two changed rows are adjacent, reconstruct your own file content from the **current** HEAD immediately before committing and re-check the parent, or commit with a pathspec (`git commit -- <paths>`), which is what finally worked.
