### 2026-08-01 — qa — T-033/PAS-13 + T-040/PAS-17: first behavioral QA pass — **FAIL, Blocker.** App crashes on every tap that opens either sheet

- **Did:** wrote `prds/hood-place-detail/TEST-PLAN.md` against both TRDs' actual requirement list before executing (per L-018), then ran it. Confirmed checkout state first: the dispatch named `a5351ed` as the pinned `passenger-code` commit, but that hash is actually a `passenger-brain` commit (the `ios-code-reviewer` fast-re-review record) — `passenger-code` HEAD is `23dd6d5` (T-040 real geometry `72f4fc7` + T-033 build `473f325` + fix pass `307e5af` + florentin blurb hand-edit `23dd6d5`), which is the tree `a5351ed`'s own review text describes verifying. Tested against `23dd6d5`, noted the discrepancy rather than silently substituting.
- **Static/data verification, independent of the running app:** re-ran a point-in-polygon script against the current bundled `hoods-tel-aviv.json`/`places-tel-aviv.json` — **9/9 places confirmed inside their real `hood_id` polygon**, confirming the prior reviewer's finding rather than trusting it. Confirmed `florentin` is the only Hood with a non-null `blurb` among all 24 (direct JSON read). Confirmed `BuildPhase.seedIsAuthoritative == true` in source. Full test suite (`PassengerTests` + `PassengerUITests`, one `xcodebuild test` invocation, not scoped) — **80 tests, 15 suites, all green**, plus `ColdOpenPerformanceTests` avg 0.460s.
- **Behavioral pass in `iPhone 17 Pro` Simulator (iOS 26.5) found what the static/unit-test pass could not:**
  - **Blocker — tapping any place pin, or the `HoodButton`, crashes the app.** `EXC_BREAKPOINT`, `Fatal error: No Observable object of type SavedPlacesStore found` (pin tap) / `...PlaceCatalog found` (HoodButton tap), both thrown from `SwiftUICore/Environment+Objects.swift:34` while SwiftUI builds the sheet content's `@Environment` property wrappers — the crash happens before `HoodSheet`/`PlaceDetailModal`'s own code ever runs. **100% reproducible on a from-scratch clean build** (deleted DerivedData, `clean build`, fresh `simctl uninstall`/`install`) — not a stale-artifact fluke, not timing-dependent (reproduces whether the tap is immediate or 5+ seconds after launch). `MapScreen.swift:126-129` applies `.environment(placeCatalog)`/`.environment(detailRouter)`/`.environment(savedPlacesStore)` immediately before `.sheet(isPresented: detailRouter.isDepth1Presented)` at line 129 — correct SwiftUI ordering on its face — yet the presented sheet cannot see any of them. This makes every one of `hood-place-detail.md`'s P0 reqs 1–7 unreachable in the running app: the Hood sheet, the place modal, the blurb branch, the empty state, both ✕ controls, and swipe-to-dismiss are all untestable and unusable. **Root cause not fully isolated** — flagged as worth investigating as a `Map` + `.sheet` + `@Observable`-environment interaction on iOS 26.5, in the same family as the already-documented FB19394663 tap-gesture quirk in this same file, but that's a lead, not a diagnosis.
  - **Major, new finding — `PlaceLayer` renders pins at every zoom, not "close zoom only."** `Map/PlaceLayer.swift` has no zoom-based visibility gate at all (unlike `HoodLayer`'s `showsName` gate off `nameLabelSpanThreshold`); `MapScreen.swift:66-68`'s `ForEach(placeCatalog.allPlaces)` wraps no zoom condition. Contradicts `hood-place-detail/TRD.md` §3.3 ("Pins render at close zoom only") and §8 D5 (describes only the *threshold value* as undecided, not whether gating exists at all). All 9 bundled places are visible, clustered into an overlapping blob per Hood, at the cold-open city-wide camera — this is also what made the crash trivially reachable on literally the first tap after launch.
  - **Why the test suite missed both:** `DetailRouterTests` exercises the router's state machine as plain method calls, never rendering `HoodSheet`/`PlaceDetailModal` inside the live `MapScreen` environment chain. No XCUITest taps a pin or Hood and asserts a sheet appears — `PassengerUITests` has only `ColdOpenPerformanceTests` (launch timing). Recommended for the fix pass: at least one UI test that taps a pin and asserts the sheet's content actually renders; this exact regression would have failed it immediately.
  - **Confirmed clean, independent of the crash:** zero network activity — `lsof -p <Passenger PID> -i` returned no TCP/UDP sockets for the app process at every checkpoint (fresh launch, mid-session, post-relaunch), consistent with `BuildPhase.seedIsAuthoritative`'s zero-fetch design, observed behaviorally not just via the unit test's spy.
- **Verdict: FAIL.** Everything gated behind opening either sheet (blurb branch, empty-Hood state, ✕ dismiss, swipe-to-dismiss, VoiceOver on sheet controls) is BLOCKED, not merely untested — the sheets never render. Full case-by-case results in `prds/hood-place-detail/TEST-PLAN.md`.
- **Left behind:** routing back to `build` per the QA lifecycle — this needs `ios-developer` to root-cause the environment-propagation failure (both crash sites and their exact fatal-error text are in the TEST-PLAN doc) and add the zoom gate `PlaceLayer` is missing, then a fresh QA pass once both land, since almost nothing in this feature was actually exercised behaviorally this round.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `prds/hood-place-detail/TEST-PLAN.md` (new), `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry), explicit paths. Committed, not pushed — pushing is Aviran-gated. Not touching `BOARD.md`/Linear per the dispatch brief.

### 2026-07-31 — ios-code-reviewer — T-033/PAS-13 code review of Build-Phase-1 slice (`473f325`): **REQUEST CHANGES** — one blocking finding, three should-fix

- **Scope:** `passenger-code` `473f325` (24 files, C1-C12+C3a) against `hood-place-detail/TRD.md` §3.4.1, §4.1, §4.3, §4.7, §4.8, §11. Read every changed file in full, not the build report's summary of them, and independently re-derived each of the six things `chief-of-staff` asked me to check.
- **Blocking — `HoodSheet.swift` has no ✕ dismiss control; the design spec explicitly requires one and this is a silent regression of an already-fixed defect.** `hood-place-detail-design.md` line 41-45's flow diagram states the Hood sheet's own exit is "Swipe down (drag handle) or ✕ → back to map," and line 72-73 states explicitly the ✕ control is "shared with the Hood sheet header" (44×44pt target, 32pt visual glyph) — this exact fix retired REJECT finding B3 (a Fitts's-Law violation) at `design-approval`, per line 158's own changelog. `HoodSheet.swift`'s header (lines 13-23) renders only `Text(hood.name)`; there is no dismiss button anywhere in the file. `PlaceDetailModal.swift` correctly builds this pattern (`closeButton`, lines 52-65) — the Hood sheet has no equivalent. The TRD's own §4.8 rendering table for `HoodSheet(hood:)` doesn't list a ✕ either, but §5's flow diagram does ("✕ / drag → closeHood → map," both exit branches) — an internal TRD inconsistency, not a documented decision to drop it (no D-numbered entry addresses this). Nothing in the build report or worklog flags this as an intentional deviation. Functionally the sheet still dismisses via drag handle, so this isn't a crash or dead-end, but it's a concrete, undisclosed divergence from an approved design spec that specifically re-introduces a defect that was already found and fixed once. Fix: add the same `closeButton` pattern `PlaceDetailModal` already has, wired to `router.closeHood()` (matches §4.1's stated rule that ✕ buttons call the verbs directly, not through the bindings) — small, mechanical, same file class as everything else in this diff.
- **Should-fix 1 — `PlaceCatalogTests`'s determinism assertion 3 doesn't test what it claims to, though it's honestly documented.** Traced `AppConfig.supabase` (`Passenger/Support/AppConfig.swift:16`, untouched by this diff): it's a `static let` that reads `Bundle.main` exactly once. `sourceStaysSeedWithPopulatedConfigPresent()` parses `SupabaseConfig-populated-fixture.plist` from the *test* bundle (`Bundle(for: FixtureBundleToken.self)`) and asserts it's well-formed — but never wires that fixture into `Bundle.main`, so `AppConfig.supabase` stays `nil` in this test exactly as it does in tests 1 and 2. Since `PlaceCatalog.load()`'s `BuildPhase.seedIsAuthoritative` branch returns before ever touching `AppConfig`/`PlacesAPI`, this test cannot structurally distinguish "pin" from "accidental fall-through because no plist exists" — the exact distinction TRD §3.4.1 says assertion 3 exists to prove. If a future change reintroduced `|| AppConfig.supabase == nil` into the `load()` short-circuit, this test would still pass in this environment (no real `SupabaseConfig.plist` is bundled into the app target), silently defeating its own purpose. The in-code comment (lines 66-78) is transparent about the real constraint (can't commit a fixture into `Bundle.main` without a permanent shipped-target file, forbidden by the no-secrets rule) and the workaround adds *some* signal (confirms the fixture itself is well-formed, re-confirms the spy isn't called with a fully-capable spy) — not fabricated, just non-additive over tests 1+2 given the current `load()` structure. Not blocking on its own since production code is correct and the gap is test-strength, not behavior, but flagged clearly per the dispatch's own emphasis: a real fix needs `AppConfig` to become bundle-injectable (mirroring `PlaceCatalog`/`HoodCatalog`'s own `bundle:` parameter) so a genuine populated-`Bundle.main` scenario is constructible — out of this diff's file scope (`AppConfig.swift` isn't touched here), so a follow-up rather than a re-dispatch.
- **Should-fix 2 — D4's "a test asserts both display strings" isn't built.** Grepped `PassengerTests/` for `PlaceCategory`/`.displayName`/`"Eat & Drink"`/`"Things to do"` — zero hits. The grep-for-retired-string half of D4/C12 is satisfied (confirmed independently, no `"Food & drinks"` anywhere), but no test exercises `PlaceCategory.displayName` directly. Trivial to add, not blocking.
- **Should-fix 3 (operational, not a defect in `473f325`) — the shared working tree currently has an uncommitted modification to `Passenger/Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` that QA must not test against, and it does not appear to actually be committed despite the entry immediately below this one describing it as done.** `git status` on `passenger-code` shows this file modified relative to `HEAD` (473f325), and `git log` shows nothing after `473f325` — so `data-engineer`'s real-Hood-geometry work (next entry below, founder-authorized, legitimate) has apparently not landed a `passenger-code` commit yet even though its own worklog entry says "commits only, per standing rule." The **committed** version at `473f325` is exactly what T-040's approved review describes and what my own coordinate check below uses: 4 Hoods, 4-point placeholder rectangles, 3 `[PROVISIONAL]` blurbs + `lev-hair` null. The **working-tree** version is data-engineer's real dataset: 24 real Tel Aviv neighborhoods, real detailed polygons (up to 650 points), and **zero blurbs on any Hood** (all `null` — the three attributes ship null/false per that entry's own note). If `qa` (or anyone) tests against the live working directory instead of a clean checkout of `473f325`, they will observe a different, larger, blurb-less Hood dataset than this review verified — breaking the req-2 blurb-branch demo and invalidating my rectangle-containment check below. Not this diff's fault and not staged by it. Flagging the apparent passenger-code-commit gap here so whoever picks up `data-engineer`'s thread checks `git log`/`git status` there directly rather than trusting the worklog's "commits only" line at face value (per this workspace's own L-009-shaped rule: an action you couldn't verify is reported as unverified, not assumed done). (`.claude/agents/marketing.md` is also modified uncommitted in this tree — unrelated, already flagged by prior entries as another session's file.)
- **The six things I was specifically asked to verify, checked against code/data directly, not the build report:**
  1. **`BuildPhase.seedIsAuthoritative` pin** — confirmed a plain `enum { static let seedIsAuthoritative = true }`, no `#if`. `PlaceCatalog.load()` checks it first and returns unconditionally on `true` before touching `api`/`cache` (`PlaceCatalog.swift:90-107`). Assertions 1 and 2 are solid and correctly test what they claim (`source == .seed` after load; a `FetchSpy` actor proven never called). Assertion 3 is the should-fix above.
  2. **`isDepth1Presented`/`isDepth2Presented`** — read `DetailRouter.swift` directly: both are computed `Binding<Bool>` with `set` routed to `closeHood()`/`closePlace()`, `set(true)` a no-op, exactly TRD §4.1's table. Traced both presentation sites: `MapScreen.swift:129` wires site A to `detailRouter.isDepth1Presented`; `HoodSheet.swift:30` wires site B to `router.isDepth2Presented`. `DetailRouterTests.swift` covers both dismiss directions plus the "writing true is ignored" case (12 tests total). No desync path found.
  3. **`places-tel-aviv.json` quality floor** — independently computed (Python, not trusted from the report): all 9 places' lat/lon fall inside their `hood_id`'s bundled rectangle in the **committed** `hoods-tel-aviv.json` (margins 0.001-0.005°, none razor-thin). 9 places (≥8), 3 Hoods carrying places + `lev-hair` at zero (≥2 Hoods, one empty). Both categories present. Real Tel Aviv establishment names (Anna Loulou Bar, Dr. Shakshuka, HaMakolet, etc.), not placeholders. `_note` field declares it provisional in-line. Floor met.
  4. **`PlaceHitTesterTests` `dx:8`→`dx:40` fix** — re-derived the unit math independently: `MKMetersPerMapPointAtLatitude(32.05°) ≈ earthCircumference·cos(32.05°)/2²⁸ ≈ 0.1266`, so `dx:8` → ~1.01m (inside the old `tolerance:2`, contradicting the original test's own "just outside" claim — confirms the bug was real) and `dx:40` → ~5.06m (correctly inside `tolerance:10`, outside `tolerance:2`). Confirmed production is unaffected by reading both call sites directly: `PlaceHitTester.place(at:tolerance:)` (`point.distance(to:)`) and `MapScreen.mapPointTolerance(forScreenPoints:...)` (also `.distance(to:)`) both use the same scaled-meters unit consistently, so the units cancel correctly in the real tap path — this was purely a test-authoring assumption, not a production bug, as claimed.
  5. **Scope cleanliness** — `git show 473f325 --name-only` matches the claimed 24 files exactly; no `database/migrations/` file touched; grepped `drift` — only the unrelated `ContrastRatioTests.swift` comment, B2's check correctly left dormant, not stubbed; grepped `"Food & drinks"` — zero hits; `Place` carries no `isTouristTrap`/`placeType`/`keywords`/`permanentlyClosed` fields (D7 held).
  6. **HIG/App Store/security pass** — ran a `/vibe-security`-equivalent check by hand: no `print`/`NSLog`/`Logger` of location or coordinates anywhere in `Places/`/`Detail/`/`Map/`; no hardcoded keys; `SavedPlacesStore` persists slugs only (`Payload { let ids: [String] }`), no coordinates/timestamps, matching §3.5. No `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes`/Waze scheme added (correct — D3, no `canOpenURL` probe needed for a system app). All new interactive targets are ≥44pt (`PlaceLayer` pin 44×44, `HoodButton` `minHeight:44`, `PlaceDetailModal`'s ✕/Save both 44×44, `HoodSheet`'s place rows `minHeight:44` and empty-state CTA has invisible padding to 44pt). VoiceOver labels present throughout ("Name, Category" on rows and pins, "Save"/"Saved" tracking state, never a checkmark). Dynamic Type: place rows and category text use semantic styles with no `.lineLimit`, correctly wrapping rather than truncating per design spec §4 — one minor exception noted below. No privacy-manifest-relevant API newly used (MapKit `openInMaps` needs no new `Info.plist` entry).
  - **One additional minor, not blocking:** `HoodButton.swift:15` sets `.lineLimit(1)` on the Hood name label — at the largest accessibility Dynamic Type sizes a long Hood name (e.g. "Kerem HaTeimanim," "Ramat Aviv Gimel") will ellipsize rather than wrap, unlike the wrap-not-truncate rule design spec §4 states for place rows/category text. `HoodButton`'s visual treatment is already flagged as undesigned engineering-default (TRD D6), so this is low-priority, but worth a look whenever `designer` gives it a real treatment.
- **The disclosed T-040-inherited deviation (both empty-branches on `lev-hair` rather than two separate Hoods) — agree this is reasonable, not a defect.** Verified `lev-hair` is genuinely the one already carrying the null blurb in the **committed** `hoods-tel-aviv.json` (confirmed above) and genuinely has zero entries in `places-tel-aviv.json`. Both req-2 branches are observed; reassigning it to satisfy "two different Hoods" would mean reopening T-040's own already-approved work for a demo-variety nicety the TRD itself labels **[ASSUMPTION]**. `ios-developer` flagged it plainly rather than silently reassigning — the right call.
- **Build/test evidence, independently re-run, not taken from the report:** `xcodebuild -scheme Passenger -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17 Pro' test` → **79 tests, 14 suites, all passed**, plus `ColdOpenPerformanceTests` (avg 0.462s, within budget) — matches the build report's 79/79 claim (their run measured 0.480s avg; both comfortably inside T-031's cold-open budget).
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES.** One blocking finding (`HoodSheet`'s missing ✕), scoped to a single small, mechanical fix in one file. The three should-fix items (determinism assertion 3's real limits, D4's missing display-string test, the uncommitted `hoods-tel-aviv.json` hazard for whoever runs `qa` next) should ideally land in the same pass since they're all small, but aren't blocking on their own. **Do not move to `qa` yet** — T-040 stays held for joint `qa` with T-033 per the existing plan; once the ✕ fix (and ideally the should-fixes) land, this needs a fast re-review of just the diff, not a full re-review.
- **Left behind:** the `AppConfig`-bundle-injectability follow-up (should-fix 1) is a real but small architectural gap worth a short ticket of its own rather than blocking this task on it. The uncommitted `hoods-tel-aviv.json`/`marketing.md` files are still unclaimed/unverified-committed in this shared tree — named here for `project-manager`/`chief-of-staff`, not staged or touched by this review.
- **Git:** `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` only, explicit path, this entry. Not touching `BOARD.md` or Linear per the dispatch brief — reporting back for `chief-of-staff` to route to `PAS-13` and advance board state. Committed, not pushed (pushing is Aviran-gated).

### 2026-07-31 — ios-developer — T-033/PAS-13 `build`: Build-Phase-1 slice landed — C1-C12 + C3a (retry, resuming a session-limit-terminated attempt)

- **Ask:** this was an explicit retry. A prior attempt on this same task died mid-run (hit a session limit, not a crash) right before its own verification step, leaving 15 files of uncommitted/untracked work in `passenger-code` and nothing committed. Dispatch was: read `PROGRESS.md`'s recent T-033/T-040 entries and `hood-place-detail/TRD.md` (§3.4.1, §4.3, §7, §8 D10, as amended `ecf7d1f`) first, then read every uncommitted file's actual content before assuming anything was missing or wrong, fix/complete only what genuinely needed it, then run the build/test verification the dead session never reached.
- **Found on inspection: the prior session's work was essentially complete and correct.** Read all 15 uncommitted/untracked files in full against the TRD before touching anything. `Place`/`PlaceCategory`/`PlaceHitTester` (C2) matched §3.2/§4.5 exactly, including the tie-break rule mirroring `HoodHitTester`'s. `PlaceCatalog`/`PlacesAPI`/`PlacesCache` (C3) had the `init(api:cache:)` seam exactly matching `DensityStore.init(api:cache:now:)`, the `BuildPhase.seedIsAuthoritative` short-circuit correctly placed before the live→cache→seed precedence, and the seed-only "keep an orphan-`hood_id` place" boundary rule correctly separated from the live-path's structural stamping (§4.3). `PlaceCatalogTests` had all three §3.4.1 determinism assertions, including a well-reasoned solution to assertion 3's real constraint (`Bundle.main` can't have a test fixture plist injected without permanently bundling a committed plist into the shipped target, which `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md`'s "no secrets in code" forbids — so the test proves the property directly: a genuine plist-shaped fixture parses to a working config, and a fully-capable fetch spy still goes uncalled). `DetailRouter`'s two-way `Binding<Bool>` dismiss routing (§4.1, the `trd-review` finding folded into D9) was correct and fully tested, including the "writing `true` is a no-op" case. `SavedPlacesStore`'s generation-guarded persistence actor (§4.4) was correct, with its own isolated-file test rig to avoid Swift Testing's concurrent-test races. `MapScreen`'s integration (place layer, tap-resolution priority place-before-Hood, `HoodButton` correctly reasoned as not-yet-bucket-2-chrome since T-032 has no nav-row state in this codebase yet, cross-task rule correctly left unwired rather than fabricated) matched §4.2/§4.5/§4.7. `DirectionsService` (C9) matched §4.6/D3 (Apple Maps only, walking mode set once, disabled branch built though unreachable). `places-tel-aviv.json` (C3a) declared itself provisional in-line, real Tel Aviv place names, both categories present, ≥8 places across 3 Hoods, `lev-hair` deliberately zero places. Nothing here needed rewriting.
- **One deviation found in C3a worth flagging, not fixed:** the TRD's quality floor asks for "one Hood deliberately carrying zero places and one deliberately carrying no blurb" — the prior session (and T-040's already-committed `hoods-tel-aviv.json`, which fixed `lev-hair` as the null-blurb Hood before this file existed) satisfies both with the *same* Hood (`lev-hair`), not two different ones. The file's own `_note` states this was deliberate ("demonstrates both of PRD req 2's empty branches at once"). This satisfies the TRD's literal pass/fail floor (both branches are observed) but means no Hood in the Phase-1 demo shows "has places, no blurb" as a distinct combination — a minor coverage gap in demo variety, not a defect. Not changed, since fixing it would mean reassigning T-040's already-reviewed-and-approved null blurb to a different Hood, which is outside this task's scope.
- **What I verified rather than took on trust:** manually checked all 9 places in `places-tel-aviv.json` fall inside their `hood_id`'s bundled placeholder rectangle in `hoods-tel-aviv.json` (all pass, one within 0.0002° of its Hood's northern edge). Grepped for `"Food & drinks"` across `.swift`/`.json` — zero hits (D4/C12). Grepped for `drift` — only an unrelated hit in `ContrastRatioTests.swift`; the B2 drift-check test was correctly left unbuilt/dormant, not stubbed green, per the TRD's explicit instruction. Confirmed via `git status` that no file under `database/migrations/` was touched (A1/A2 correctly out of scope) and `.claude/agents/marketing.md` (another session's in-flight edit) was left untouched.
- **Ran the verification the dead session never reached:** `xcodebuild -scheme Passenger -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17 Pro' build` (no iPhone 16 on this machine, same substitution T-040's build used) → **BUILD SUCCEEDED**. `xcodebuild … test` (both test targets) → **one genuine failure found**: `PlaceHitTesterTests.withinToleranceNearMiss()`. Root-caused before fixing, not guessed at: wrote a standalone Swift script confirming `MKMapPoint.distance(to:)` returns *geographic meters* (scaled by `MKMetersPerMapPointAtLatitude`, ≈0.126 at this test's Tel-Aviv-anchored latitude), not a raw Euclidean count of map-point units — the test's `dx: 8` offset produced an actual distance of ~1.01, which is *inside* `tolerance: 2`, contradicting the test's own "just outside tolerance misses" assertion. **Confirmed this is a test-authoring bug, not a production bug:** `PlaceHitTester.place(at:tolerance:)` and `MapScreen.mapPointTolerance(forScreenPoints:...)` both use `.distance(to:)` consistently, so the units cancel out correctly in the real tap-resolution path — this was purely the test's own numeric assumption being wrong. ### 2026-07-31 — ios-code-reviewer — T-040/PAS-17 `trd-review`/code review of the Build-Phase-1 slice (C1/C2/C2a): APPROVE with minor notes

- **Scope:** reviewed `passenger-code` `3d94ba6` against `hood-dataset/TRD.md` §3.5, §4.2, §4.3, §8 D10, §11 (as amended `ecf7d1f`) — `Hood.swift`, `HoodCatalog.swift`, `Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json`, the two new fixtures, `HoodCatalogTests.swift`, `HoodHitTesterTests.swift`. Read `ios-developer`'s worklog (`607245e`) and `chief-of-staff`'s verification entry directly above this one, then re-derived every claim from the diff myself rather than trusting either summary.
- **Decode logic, verified correct, not just present:** `schemaVersion >= 1` gate (§4.3) — accepts unknown higher versions, throws below 1, both directions tested (`acceptsSchemaVersion2`, `schemaVersionBelowOneThrows` against the new `schemaVersion: 0` fixture). Blurb `""`/whitespace → `nil` via `trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespacesAndNewlines)`, correct and tested against both an empty string and a whitespace-only string in the same fixture. Absent-centroid fallback: traced the actual order of operations — ring dedup (drop trailing duplicate when `first == last`) happens **before** the averaged-centroid computation, so the fallback centroid is computed from the same deduped/open point set the ring itself uses, matching the doc comment's claim that leaving the duplicate in would "double-weight one corner." Explicit bundle-provided centroid is honored over recompute (tested, with a fixture value deliberately differing from the vertex average so the test can't pass by accident). The raw `entry.polygon.count >= 3` guard runs before dedup and the `coordinates.count >= 3` guard runs after, so a degenerate closed ring (e.g. 3 raw points, 2 distinct) still throws rather than silently passing — matches the worklog's claim, confirmed by reading the guard order, not assumed.
- **Fixture geometry, confirmed byte-identical:** read the actual diff hunk on `hoods-tel-aviv.json` — every polygon coordinate line is unchanged; the only additions are `schemaVersion` 1→2, a new top-level `_note`, and a `blurb` key appended after each closing `]`. No coordinate touched.
- **The two flagged implementation choices — both reasonable:** (a) picking `lev-hair` as the arbitrary null-blurb Hood is fine — TRD/board text says "one of them," not which one, and the choice preserves the load-bearing property (T-033's no-blurb branch stays observable). (b) The `[PROVISIONAL]` inline string prefix plus a top-level `_note` key is a reasonable way to hand-author "this is provisional" into a schema with no comment syntax, and confirmed harmless to `Decodable` — Swift's synthesized decode ignores JSON keys absent from `CodingKeys`, verified by reading `CatalogFile`/`Entry`'s member lists against the JSON, not taken on the developer's word.
- **Scope discipline confirmed independently:** `git show --stat 3d94ba6` — exactly the 7 files the worklog names, no more. `git show 3d94ba6 --name-only | grep -i migrat` — no hits. `grep -rn "Hood(id:"` across the repo — exactly the two construction call sites the worklog claims (`HoodCatalog.load()`, `HoodHitTesterTests`'s two factory helpers), both updated correctly for the new memberwise initializer. No B1/B2/B4/B6/C3/C4/C5/A1 work present.
- **HIG/App Store/security pass (same standard as T-031/T-033):** this diff adds no view, no UI, no network call, and no new consumer of the three fields (confirmed by the doc comment and by `grep` — nothing outside `Hoods/`/`PassengerTests/` references `blurb`/`isTouristTrap`/`designatedForProgression` yet). No location or personal data touched — static reference data only, matching §3.5's own claim. No secrets. Nothing here can trigger an App Store or privacy-manifest finding on its own.
- **Two minor notes (non-blocking):** (1) nothing programmatically stops a `[PROVISIONAL]`-prefixed blurb from surviving into a real build if Phase 2 (C3/B6) slips — the only safeguard is the human-readable `_note` field, which `Decodable` ignores and no test reads. (2) the new malformed-centroid-array-length throw path (`centroidPair.count == 2` guard) has no test, unlike the other three new decode branches which are each covered — a small coverage gap on a genuine boundary-validation branch, flagged for whoever next touches `HoodCatalogTests.swift`.
- **Verdict: APPROVE with minor notes.** Both notes above are should-fix, not blocking — move to `qa` for behavioral verification (T-033's Hood-sheet blurb rendering against this bundle is the area that most deserves a look, since that's the actual reason this carve-out exists).
- **Left behind:** the two minor notes above, for whoever next touches this surface (likely C3/B6 at Build Phase 2, or a `qa` pass in the interim). Did not touch `BOARD.md` or Linear per the brief — reporting back for whoever dispatched this to advance state and post to `PAS-17`.

  - `Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` (C2a): `schemaVersion` 1 → 2. Geometry byte-identical to before (diffed to confirm). ### 2026-07-31 — product — T-032/PAS-15: `design-approval` verdict — REJECT (product half); PRD amended to Draft v5

- **Did:** the `product` half of T-032's two-signature `design-approval` gate, against PRD Draft v4 (`prds/time-slider/time-slider.md`), design spec v2 (`design/phase-1/time-slider-design.md`) and the live mockup. **Verdict: REJECT → back to `design`**, 2 blocking + 1 should-fix. Did **not** touch `BOARD.md`'s state field (`chief-of-staff`'s to advance); added my verdict as row content only.
- **I could reach the mockup, and it matters.** `chief-of-staff` recorded that the artifact URL 404s/sign-in-walls in its session and signed off relying on `designer`'s claims. It resolves fine from mine — `WebFetch` returned 67KB of published source, which I read in full rather than fetching a rendered view. So that gap is an auth-context difference between sessions, not a dead link, and someone in this pipeline **can** independently verify these artifacts. Worth knowing before the next one gets waved through on the author's own testimony.
- **Blocking 1 — the new edge track's inactive rail misses the bar the spec asserts for it.** Spec §4/§7 state the custom-drawn edge rail is "held to the full 3:1 bar, not exempted" — correct, since PRD req 6 conditions that exemption on a *platform-drawn* control. The published mockup then draws `.edge-track-line` as `background: var(--surface-3)`, byte-identical to the horizontal `.track-line` that only survives review *because* it is exempt. Recomputed from the artifact's own `:root` hex values (`#E8E2D6` on `#FFFFFF`; `#2C3034` on `#1A1C1F`): **1.29:1 light / 1.28:1 dark** against a 3:1 bar. Those are the exact two numbers already sitting in this PRD's Decisions log for the horizontal rail. §4 enumerated the stop-dot (6.12:1/7.45:1) and the fill (3.93:1/6.62:1) — both genuinely pass — and called that the rail. **The same enumeration failure the v1 pass was rejected for**, one control over. Handed `designer` a computed fix rather than just the defect: `--fg-faint` gives 3.22:1 / 3.61:1, clearing 3:1 in both themes with no new token.
- **Blocking 2 — 24pt edge capture zone vs. req 6's ≥44pt floor, never reconciled.** §7 cites Fitts's Law to justify 24pt; `design-principles.md` §2 states that law *as* "≥44pt touch targets," flat, no screen-edge exception. The 24pt is argued from MapKit pan arbitration and claimed map surface — real constraints, but architectural, not accessibility. §7 also frames it as "the same drawn-smaller-than-target pattern" as the thumb (28pt visible / 44pt frame) — but the thumb's *hit* frame meets 44pt and the zone's does not, so the one number req 6 measures is precisely the one that differs, and a reviewer skimming that row would read it as an already-approved pattern.
- **My own half of blocking 2, done in the same commit (L-009).** Req 6's touch-target bullet was written when there was one control and was never re-scoped when v2/v3 added a structurally different second one — so no gate had an objective pass condition for the edge zone, exactly the blind spot L-009 exists to close. Split it per path in the PRD (button/modal frame ≥44pt on the drag axis; edge zone ≥44pt on its acquisition axis **or** an explicitly-reasoned departure Aviran ratifies), and opened **Q7** routing that departure to his `design-review` gate. **Deliberately did not lower the floor myself** — that's cutting a requirement to make a gate pass, which is an escalation, not a `product` call. I did give the argument I think makes it ratifiable, for `designer` to make properly: an edge-anchored target can't be overshot past the bezel, the gesture axis is ~688–708pt, and req 6's own bullet keeps all 13 hours selectable without the gesture, so a user who can't acquire a 24pt band keeps a conforming path.
- **Should-fix, non-blocking:** §1's Q3 note points to "§5b," which is Q6 — there is no Q3 section, and Q3 appears nowhere in §8's open items either. The one question that is genuinely Aviran's could therefore reach his gate only via the PRD, not via the spec he'll be reading alongside the mockup.
- **Verified good, from source, not from the worklog:** all 7 reqs have a §5 traceability row; `setHour()` really is the single writer both paths call, so Q3's one-shared-value is structural rather than two synced copies; `hourFromClientY` is one shared function (`1 - (clientY-top)/height`), so up=later holds identically on both edges by construction, not by a matching pair of handlers; `startEdgeDrag` gates on `state.view !== 'map'` (Q6) and `edgeLive(side)` (Q2) **in JS**, not merely by CSS/z-order; absolute-position (not delta) mapping does make every stop reachable from any touch-down point (req 1's widened bullet); the readout is positioned against `#mapArea`'s rect, so the ~10px bug `designer` reported catching is genuinely fixed in the published file; edge elements carry `aria-hidden`, matching §4's inert-to-AT claim. `fg-muted`/`heat` on `surface` recompute to 6.12:1 and 3.93:1, matching the spec exactly — the designer's numbers are trustworthy across the board, which is why blocking 1 reads as an enumeration gap rather than carelessness.
- **Scope gate (Job 0), re-run on inherited work, clean.** Strategy line 35 quoted verbatim — "Time slider, now → +12 hours. A place's relevance changes by the hour; the map knows that. Unchanged." — matches the PRD's Motivation word-for-word. No requirement *text* changed at v4. No social surface, no onboarding, no Phase-2 hook: notably the edge idle hint is explicitly the *answer* to discoverability without an onboarding surface (§7, citing strategy's own "no onboarding" line), which honors the prohibition rather than routing around it. `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` not touched.
- **L-013 check:** re-read both 2026-07-31 FOUNDER-DIRECT STUBs rather than the summaries. Verbatim "Supplement (recommended)" and "both edges. its not swipe from left to right..you slide the you finder from up to down on one of the edges" are carried faithfully into the PRD; the "one continuous motion" reading is still correctly labelled inference, and Q3 is still correctly *not* treated as answered. No gloss found that the stubs hadn't already labelled.
- **Data/schema rule (Job 1 item 7):** re-checked, third confirmation — no new table, column, endpoint or query parameter, and neither the edge gesture nor this design pass adds one. No supporting data PRD warranted.
- **Left behind:** T-032 needs a `designer` fix pass on findings 1–2 and then a **fresh** `design-approval` (both signatures again) — `chief-of-staff`'s APPROVE was given against the artifact as it stands and blocking 1 is a defect in that artifact. Q7 must ride into the `design-review` packet alongside Q3; both are Aviran's, and Q7 is new. `T-045` (10 uncommitted `agents-mirror/*.md`, 2 untracked `design/*.md`) still unowned — refused again here per safety rule 2, still `project-manager`'s.

Mine are comparisons of the spec *against* things it doesn't cite: the PRD's own req 7 text, `design-principles.md`'s stated number, and the spec's own preserved v1 sentences. Complementary, not conflicting — the architect/data-engineer mutual-TRD-review precedent working as intended.
- **CONCURRENCY (L-007) — a second `product`-labelled session ran this same review in parallel, and I found it mid-pass, not from a relay.** `git status` showed `prds/time-slider/time-slider.md` modified in the shared tree; the diff was a **Draft v5** amendment recording a `design-approval` REJECT — req 6's touch-target bullet split per path, a new **Q7** routing the sub-44pt departure to Aviran, and a blocking contrast defect on the edge track. Same verdict, reached independently, with a real finding I had not reached (their contrast defect) and without three that I had. **I did not stage, edit or revert that file** (safety rule 2 — another session's in-flight work), and its v5 changes stand as written; my B3 below is satisfied on the PRD side by their split and Q7. **`chief-of-staff` needs to confirm only one relay goes back to `designer`** — two `product` sessions were dispatched against one gate, the same shape as the T-030 and nav-row near-collisions already on this log.
- **Scope gate re-run on inherited work (Job 0 item 4): clean.** Strategy's authorizing line verified verbatim at `strategy/passenger-strategy.md:35` — "Time slider, now → +12 hours. A place's relevance changes by the hour; the map knows that. Unchanged." No social surface, no Phase 2/3 hooks, and no onboarding surface: the spec's §9 explicitly rejects coach-marks and tutorials, which is the right posture against `strategy/passenger-strategy.md:39`.
- **Verified rather than read: no requirement text changed at v4.** `git diff b4baa33 38c2fd6` — the whole diff is the Status line, Scope note, Technical design's open-question (b), the Open questions restructure and the Decisions log. Reqs 1–7 bullets byte-identical. The header's narrower claim ("only the Open questions/Decisions log") is slightly wide of the diff; the load-bearing half is true. **Q3 verified still open, still [ASSUMPTION], untouched.**
- **B1 (blocking) — contrast on the two new edge elements is computed against a background they don't sit on.** §4 and §10 price the edge active track's stop-dot/fill and the readout chip against `--surface` (6.12:1 / 7.45:1 and 3.93:1 / 6.62:1). Those pairs are valid inside the modal card; both edge elements are drawn **over live map**. §2 gives an opaque backing to the **idle hint only** (line 98) — never to the active track (line 99) or the chip. The spec's own inherited T-031 D1 reasoning is that contrast against unknown map content isn't a checkable number, so these figures don't support the bar they're cited for. Compounding it: `designer`'s own verification records the idle hint at **opacity 0.92**, i.e. translucent over live map — precisely what §2 says it must never be. Same class as the v1 REJECT's finding 1 (a bar asserted, the actual rendered context unchecked), one layer out.
- **B2 (blocking) — req 7's sheet bullet is half-covered.** The PRD requires two things while a sheet is presented: the edge slide is unavailable **and** "an edge drag over a presented sheet moves the sheet, as it does today." §1.2 and §3's "Edge unavailable — sheet presented" state only the first — "does nothing" — and never say the touch reaches the sheet; the mockup check proved only that `state.hour` doesn't move. A build that reads "does nothing" as "the zone captures and no-ops" regresses `HoodStubSheet`'s already-shipped drag-to-dismiss inside two 24pt bands. Q6 exists to name a winner in exactly this collision, so leaving it unstated defeats the call it adopted.
- **B3 (blocking, PRD half already fixed by the concurrent pass) — req 6's "touch target ≥44pt" against the 24pt capture zone.** `design-principles.md:28` states Fitts's Law as "≥44pt touch targets" flat, with no screen-edge exception; §7 cites that same principle as the authority for half its number, and both justifications offered (win arbitration against MapKit's pan recognizer, minimize the permanently-unpannable band) are architectural — neither is about a finger acquiring the target. The spec scoped req 1's reachability bullet and req 6's rail exemption per path with real care and did not do it here. Design-side action stands: state the departure and the actual argument for it (an edge-anchored target can't be overshot past the bezel; the gesture axis is ~708pt), so Aviran has something concrete to ratify at `design-review` under their new Q7.
- **B4 (blocking) — the persistent idle hint contradicts the spec's own preserved text and the PRD's Description.** §2/§1.2 introduce "a minimal, persistent affordance at rest on each live edge"; §3's preserved v1 row still reads "no persistent chrome for it," and the PRD Description says "The edge control is not permanent chrome; it comes with the slide and goes again." `ux-flows.md:314` enumerates the always-on map chrome and `:83` defines the two chrome classes T-032's own v1 REJECT pass centralized — the hint belongs in both and appears in neither. **I deliberately did not pre-emptively widen the PRD Description to let this pass:** amending "not permanent chrome" into permitting permanent chrome so a design clears the gate is adjacent to cutting a requirement to make something pass, which is Aviran's call, not mine (agent file, Escalate). The hint may well be the right answer — it needs to be reconciled and shown to him as a deliberate addition of permanent map chrome, not arrive as an unremarked side effect.
- **Should-fix (6):** §5's traceability table claims full coverage but only P0-6/P0-7 were revisited per path — P0-1…P0-5 rows still point at button-path-only v1 sections (the behaviour *is* covered in §1.2/§2/§3's edge rows, so the table is stale rather than the coverage absent, which is why it isn't blocking); P0-1's "no elastic overshoot" has no stated clamp rule where absolute-position mapping meets the 64pt/40pt insets, exactly where a dragging finger will travel; the idle hint is the entire discoverability answer and the one new element given no visibility figure; the discoverability argument needs its missing sentence (below); the spec header still reads "PRD: (Draft v3)" though this pass drove the PRD to v4 and now v5; §5a silently normalizes the founder's verbatim quote ("finder" → "finger") in a project that has L-013 specifically because quotes drift — quote exactly or mark `[sic]`.
- **My own product judgment on the discoverability answer (the thing I flagged at v2, asked for directly in the brief):** the idle hint is a genuine answer, and it is not a complete one. It makes the control **noticeable**, not **legible** — a 5pt capsule says "something is here," never "slide vertically to move the hour." What rescues it is cheap recovery (one exploratory touch brings up the full track with its numeral) plus Q1 landing on **supplement**, so a user who never touches an edge still reaches every hour through the heat button. That is the real de-risking, and it is missing from §7's row, which leans on Fogg B=MAT alone — B=MAT explains why a hint beats a coach-mark, not why it's sufficient. One sentence fixes it. I'm not asking for more UI, and specifically not for anything onboarding-shaped.
- **Also named, not blocking:** Q3's cost is understated — the design is *structurally* one shared value (one position-to-hour function, one write target, §5/§7), so "two independent sliders" would not be a tweak to this spec; Aviran should see that framing when he answers, not just the mockup demonstration. And the PRD grew **3,435 → 4,199 words** at a pass that closed two open questions and changed no requirements — against my own v3 prediction that closing them would shrink it. The resolutions were written as essays inside Open questions instead of collapsing into the Decisions log. I did not compress it this pass (the doc is another session's in-flight file, and mid-loop churn on a document under review helps nobody); it is compressible by roughly the Q1/Q2/Q4/Q5 branches once Q3 and Q7 land, and I'll do it in the same commit as the acceptance status flip (L-006).
- **Left behind:** four blocking findings back to `designer`; B3's PRD half already done by the concurrent session, its design half still open; `chief-of-staff` to reconcile the double dispatch and post to Linear `PAS-15` (no Linear write access here). `T-045` (uncommitted `agents-mirror/*.md` + untracked `design/*.md`) still unclaimed, still `project-manager`'s — refused again, not swept in.
- **Safety rule 2, observed live rather than theorised — commit `0dd3d21` swept my in-flight `BOARD.md` row.** That commit ("T-032 design-approval: product verdict REJECT; PRD to Draft v5") contains **my** T-032 row text verbatim, down to its "3 blocking, 6 should-fix" count, because it staged `agent-os/BOARD.md` whole out of this shared working tree while my edit was sitting in it. Recording it because this is precisely the hazard rule 2 describes, caught in the act: **stage explicit paths and re-check the diff of a shared file before committing it, not just the path list.**

Two `chief-of-staff` entries (`d82b55c`, `08b07e2`) committed while this pass was running; both describe work that preceded this reply and both say they are waiting on it. Left in place rather than resequenced — reordering a file two sessions are writing to is a clobber risk for zero information gain.
- **Ask:** implement `product`'s already-made Build-Phase-1 scoping call (`30e002f`) in T-033's TRD, and write the ratified T-040 carve-out into T-040's TRD. **No scoping decision of my own was made** — both calls were already taken upstream (`product` for the source pin, `chief-of-staff` for the carve-out routing); this pass is authorship, and every decision block says whose call it records.
- **Read before writing, not summarised from the dispatch:** the PRD's Draft-v2 Decisions log and Build-Phase-1 bullet, both TRDs in full, `BOARD.md`'s `V1 Build Phases` section, and the four most recent T-033/T-040 worklog entries. Also verified the two load-bearing facts directly rather than taking them from the brief: `passenger-code/Passenger/Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` is `schemaVersion` 1 with exactly `id`/`name`/`polygon` on four Hoods (`florentin`, `kerem-hateimanim`, `neve-tzedek`, `lev-hair`) and **no `blurb` anywhere**; **no `SupabaseConfig.plist` exists anywhere in `passenger-code/`** (`find`, not assumed).
- **T-033 — the pin is a constant, not an absence, and that is the whole point of the determinism requirement.** New **§3.4.1**: `BuildPhase.seedIsAuthoritative = true` short-circuits `PlaceCatalog.load()` to the bundled seed with no fetch attempted. Deliberately a **runtime constant, not `#if`** — both branches stay compiled, type-checked and greppable, so the live path can be reviewed in Phase 1 and the Phase-2 flip is one boolean in one file. Deliberately **not** "there's no plist, so it falls through": that is machine-dependent, holds on a machine that never had credentials and silently stops holding on one that does — which is exactly the accidental-pass `product` asked to rule out.
- **The determinism requirement is three pass/fail assertions, not a behavioural note.** In `PlaceCatalogTests`: (1) `source == .seed` after `load()`; (2) **zero** fetch attempts, proven by a `PlacesFetching` spy that fails the test if called at all; (3) assertion 1 **still holds with a populated `SupabaseConfig.plist` present**, supplied by the test — the assertion that distinguishes a pin from a fall-through, and the one that fails if someone later removes the constant as dead code. Assertion 2 needed a seam, so §3.4.1 adds `PlaceCatalog.init(api:cache:)` with protocol defaults — **the shipped `DensityStore.init(api:cache:now:)` idiom, read from the source, not a new pattern.** This is the only contract change in §4 this pass makes.
- **The built-but-unexercised gap is named up front, in two places.** New lead-in to **§4.3** and a new **§7** bullet: the `GET`, the `hoodID`-stamping decoder, the two live-only boundary rules, `PlacesCache`'s disk round trip, **and** the `.live`/`.cache`/`.unavailable` half of §4.8's error-vs-empty distinction (derived from `source`, which can only read `.seed` in Phase 1) all ship built and never run against a server. Phase-1 acceptance covers PRD reqs 1–7 against the fixture and covers none of that. Stated as T-031's "heat never observed live" shape explicitly, since that one had to be retroactively re-read as intentional at acceptance — naming it now is the entire point.
- **Fixture spec'd as two files with pass/fail floors, and the emitter conflict across phases reconciled.** §3.4.1 carries `product`'s floors verbatim in substance (real Tel Aviv names, both categories, coordinates inside the placeholder rectangle each `hood_id` names, ≥8 places / ≥2 Hoods, one Hood with zero places, one with no blurb, provisional declared in-file) with the numeric floors kept labelled **[ASSUMPTION]** as product's own judgment, not a founder threshold. New step **C3a** authors it. **A real conflict the brief did not name and I had to resolve:** §11 **B2** says "T-033 does not emit `places-tel-aviv.json` — T-042's B3 is the sole emitter", but T-042's B3 does not exist in Phase 1. Reconciled explicitly in B2 rather than left for the build agent to trip over: in Phase 1 C3a hand-authors the file and the drift check is **dormant — do not stub it green**; at Phase 2 B3's export overwrites it and the check goes live. The sole-emitter rule is unchanged, it simply has no emitter to be sole among yet.
- **T-040 — carve-out written in, bounded on purpose.** New **§8 D10** (decision + provenance + why exactly three steps and not more), **§3.5** (the hand-edited-bundle exception), **§11** (per-step build-phase column, new **C2a**). Two judgment calls inside the carve-out, both flagged rather than silently taken: **(a) C1 is carved whole, not blurb-only** — `isTouristTrap`, `designatedForProgression` and the stored `centroid` are all optional-with-fallback at decode per §4.3, so carrying them costs nothing and splitting one struct across two phases costs a second edit of the same file; C2a therefore adds **only** `blurb` and the `schemaVersion` bump, with the other three left absent and **explicitly must not be invented**. **(b) C4's three decode assertions ride forward with C2** (`schemaVersion` 2 accepted, `""`→`nil`, absent `centroid`→averaged fallback) — a decode rule shipped without the test that proves it is a rule nobody checked. That second one is **my own bounded call, not in the dispatch**; C4's non-overlap tripwire stays Phase 2 with the real geometry it exists to check. B1/B2/B4/B6, A1 and C3–C5 stay Phase 2 exactly as held.
- **The hand-edited bundle is a stated exception, with a named retirement.** T-040's D2/§5.3 make the generator the sole emitter and `HoodCatalogTests` the tripwire for a hand-edited bundle. §3.5 now authorises exactly one exception — geometry untouched, `blurb` + `schemaVersion` only — and names **C3** as what overwrites it wholesale and restores the rule. Without that paragraph, C2a reads as a violation of the same TRD's own D2.
- **Nothing else moved, and I checked rather than assumed.** No requirement added, cut or rescoped in either document; no schema, no migration, no RLS, no contract in T-040's §4; T-033's §4 gains only the injection seam. Both TRDs' `trd-review` approvals (6/6 each) stand — this is a build-order split and a source pin over already-approved steps, not new design. Both files' markdown tables re-checked for column consistency after editing (T-040's C5 row needed the new column added; caught and fixed).
- **Did:** amended `prds/hood-place-detail/TRD.md` (header, §3.4 lead, new §3.4.1, §4.3 ×2, §7, new §8 D10, §11 C3/C3a/B2) and `prds/hood-dataset/TRD.md` (header, §3.5, new §8 D10, §11 both tracks + new C2a). Updated `BOARD.md`: T-033's build-phase row and main row (hold cleared → `build`-ready), T-040's build-phase row and main row (carve-out marked done, C2a added), and the "Known tension" paragraph marked resolved on both halves with the original text preserved below it per convention.

### 2026-07-31 — product — T-034/PAS-25: "launch-blocking" line corrected — gate re-attributed to the pipeline (T-043, Build Phase 3), client unblocked for Build Phase 1

- **Did:** amended `prds/live-events-overlay/live-events-overlay.md` to Draft v2. Two Decisions log rows appended. No requirement text (reqs 1–6, P1s) changed.
- **The dispatch premise needed one correction before I could act on it, and it changes what the fix is.** The brief said the "launch-blocking" line "is simply wrong as currently written." The *claim* is not wrong and is not the PM's invention — it is `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` line 46, near-verbatim: "V1 does not ship until data-engineer has a working live-events ingestion pipeline (scoping ticket PAS-5)." That file is Aviran-gated (safety rule 8), so deleting or softening the gate from the PRD would have been an unauthorized scope change laundered through a "small text fix." Fixed by disambiguating, not by removing. The strategy doc was not edited and does not need to be — nothing in it is inaccurate.

No client requirement needs the real pipeline to be exercisable. Confirmed.

So the honest statement is: the client is unblocked for Phase 1 **provided a bundled fake event set exists**. It does not exist, and nobody owns it. Named explicitly in the Dependencies bullet and logged as a Decisions row rather than left as an implied assumption a developer discovers at build time (Job 1 item 7). Judged small enough to stay in this PRD rather than warrant its own supporting PRD — it is one fixture file against an already-specced schema, not a sourcing/pipeline job; that is the substantive contrast with `T-043`, which does have its own PRD.

No social surface, no onboarding, nothing reaching into parked Phase 2/3 scope. This edit adds no feature and cuts no requirement; it is a build-sequencing correction inside already-authorized V1 scope, which is exactly what `BOARD.md`'s § V1 Build Phases says that section is allowed to do.

The sibling half of the same board tension — T-033's TRD fetching T-042's/T-040's real Supabase tables — is **not** addressed here and is still open; it is an architect-side TRD change, not a PRD text fix, and nothing in this entry resolves it. `BOARD.md` and Linear `PAS-25` deliberately untouched per the brief — the T-034 row still carries the stale "PRD still calls itself launch-blocking on PAS-5" note and now needs updating by whoever owns the board.

### 2026-07-31 — product — T-033/PAS-13: Build-Phase-1 scoping call — bundled seed is authoritative, live fetch built-but-inert, fixture is a two-file data need

- **Ask:** confirm (or replace) the reading left in `hood-place-detail/TRD.md` §8 D9 — that a Build-Phase-1 T-033 needs a which-source-is-authoritative decision plus a real fixture, not a re-architecture. Verified the four cited sections myself (§3.4, §4.3, §7, step C3) rather than taking D9's summary on faith.
- **The three facts check out, verbatim.** §3.4: "Precedence when the catalog loads: **live fetch → last-good disk cache → bundled seed → empty**," and "The seed is a **floor, never a cache**," with a `.seed` case on `PlaceCatalog.Source` so a reviewer can tell which path produced what. §7: "**The client ships independently of the backend** … demoable, reviewable, and QA-able before either migration is applied or a single row exists." §4.3 additionally states "**A missing plist is a valid state**: the fetch reports unconfigured, the catalog falls through to cache, then to the bundled seed." Step C3 builds "the live→cache→seed→empty precedence and the `.seed` source case." No `SupabaseConfig.plist` exists in `passenger-code/` today (checked). So the no-backend path is genuinely designed in — `architect`'s reading is correct and I confirm it.
- **Verdict: CONFIRMED, with one correction of scope.** For Build Phase 1 the bundled seed is authoritative; the live fetch is **built but inert**, not deleted. Deleting it would make the seed the only source and turn Phase 2 into a re-architecture — the opposite of the intent. What Phase 1 needs added is *determinism and a pass/fail bullet*: a Phase-1 build must land on `PlaceCatalog.source == .seed` deterministically, on a machine that has never had credentials, asserted by a test — not merely fall through by accident of an absent plist, which behaves differently on a developer machine that happens to hold config. Mechanism is `architect`'s; the required outcome is the assertable source value. Note for §7: this is a build-phase source pin, not the feature flag §7 rules out (its off-state isn't a broken feature).
- **The correction — the fixture is two files, not one, and the second one is the load-bearing half.** `Resources/places-tel-aviv.json` doesn't exist and must be authored. But `Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` **exists at `schemaVersion` 1 with no `blurb` field** (read it: four placeholder rectangles, ids `florentin`/`kerem-hateimanim`/`neve-tzedek`/`lev-hair`, geometry only). §3.4's 2026-07-31 amendment moved the bundled blurb *out* of the places file and into the hoods file — correctly, one field one home — but the hoods file only gains `blurb` via **T-040's** `schemaVersion` 2 (its TRD §3.5, steps C1–C3), and T-040 is HELD to Build Phase 2. Net effect if nobody catches it: a Phase-1 demo that can only render req 2's **no-blurb** branch. The blurb is half of this feature's own authorizing strategy line ("Tap a Hood → hand-curated blurb + tagged spots," verified verbatim at `strategy/passenger-strategy.md:37`) and decision #10's entire "local read." Shipping the Phase-1 demo without it demos half the sentence.
- **Recommended route for the blurb, bounded deliberately:** carve **T-040 C1 + C2 only** into Build Phase 1 (`Hood` gains `blurb`, decode with the `""`/whitespace→`nil` normalisation) plus hand-authored provisional blurb values in the existing bundle. Those two steps are pure client decode with zero backend surface — Build-Phase-1-shaped by Aviran's own split. **T-040's B1/B2/B4/B6 (validator, generator, real geometry authoring, migration `003`) stay Phase 2** — I checked, and pulling the generated bundle forward would drag B4's real-geometry long pole, which *is* Phase 2. T-040's C3 ("swap in B6's bundle") stays Phase 2 and is what overwrites the provisional values. **Not decided by me** — it resequences T-040's steps, not T-033's, so it's `chief-of-staff`'s to route. **Not a build blocker for T-033**: §4.8/C7 already render blurb-when-present with both branches specced, so the values can land in parallel. It *is* an acceptance-scope condition — Phase-1 acceptance of req 2 requires the blurb branch be observable, not just the empty one.
- **Fixture quality bar, stated as pass/fail so `qa` has something to fail (L-009).** "Fake data" with no floor produces a fixture that passes construction and fails the demo. Required: real Tel Aviv place names (not "Place 1"), both categories present, and **each place's coordinate inside the bundled polygon its `hood_id` names** — the bundled Hoods are placeholder rectangles, so coordinates are snapped, not real; real coordinates would scatter pins outside the coloured shapes and read as broken. Floors: ≥8 places across ≥2 Hoods, one Hood deliberately with **zero** places, one deliberately with **no blurb**, so req 2's two empty branches are observed rather than asserted. Fixture declares itself provisional in-file. All four floors are **[ASSUMPTION]** — product judgment for a demo that doesn't read as a toy, not a founder threshold; the empty/blurb-less pair is the part worth keeping regardless.
- **No separate supporting PRD for the fixture, and that's a judgment call I'm naming rather than skipping.** ~10–15 rows plus 2–3 short blurbs, snapped to four placeholder rectangles, discarded wholesale at Phase 2 — that is a build-time fixture with owners already assigned (T-042 owns the emitter, T-040 owns the blurb field), not a sourcing/authoring/pipeline deliverable with its own quality stakes. Specified in T-033's PRD Technical design instead.
- **Nothing in this PRD defers to Phase 2.** Walked all 7 P0s: Hood sheet open/content/empty states, place modal content, primary-action hierarchy, route hand-off (Apple Maps per D3), two-category enforcement, save. Every one is demoable and QA-able against a fixture. Phase 1 cuts no requirement — worth stating, because the failure mode here is quietly narrowing acceptance to what the fixture happens to cover.
- **Carried-forward gap, named up front rather than discovered at Phase-2 acceptance:** §4.3's live path — the embedded `GET`, the `hoodID`-stamped-from-enclosing-Hood decoder, the live-only boundary rules, the disk cache — ships **built and unexercised** in Phase 1. Phase-1 acceptance does not cover it and must not claim to. It needs its own verification when Phase 2 wires the real tables. This is the same shape as T-031's "heat never observed live," which had to be retroactively re-read as intentional; naming it now avoids the re-read.
- **L-013 flag, and it matters here.** The only verbatim Aviran words on record are *"yes, replan board around the 3 phases."* Every specific — "100% client-side," "no backend," "fake data" — is `chief-of-staff`'s relayed restatement, already self-labelled **[ASSUMPTION]** in its own FOUNDER-DIRECT STUB. I therefore did **not** read "no backend" as "no networking code may exist"; I read it as "must not depend on Supabase being reachable," which building-but-inerting satisfies. If Aviran means the stricter reading, that's his call and it changes C3 — flagging rather than assuming, since reasoning from a relayed adjective is exactly the PAS-11 "curated" failure.
- **Did:** amended `prds/hood-place-detail/hood-place-detail.md` to Draft v2 — corrected the now-wrong "hard upstream … unfalsifiable against an empty dataset" line (struck, not deleted), added a Build-Phase-1 data-need bullet naming both fixture files and the quality floors, appended a Decisions log row. **No requirement text touched** — this sequencing adds, cuts and rescopes nothing.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md`, Linear, `TRD.md`, or `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` (Aviran-gated; nothing here needed it — the authorizing line already exists and this changes no scope). Did not stage other agents' in-flight files in this shared tree (Safety rule 2).
- **Left behind:** `architect` amends §3.4/§4.3/§7/C3 per the above. `chief-of-staff` routes the T-040 C1+C2 carve-out question and decides whether to put the stricter "no networking at all" reading in front of Aviran.
- **Git:** committed to `passenger-brain` (`main`), explicit paths — `prds/hood-place-detail/hood-place-detail.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md`. **Committed, not pushed.**
- **Correction, same entry, stated rather than silently fixed:** I first wrote the git line above as "`git remote -v` empty on this repo, checked this turn." **That was wrong — I wrote the boilerplate before running the check.** `passenger-brain` now HAS a remote: `origin https://github.com/AviranGrisaro/passenger-brain.git`, `.git/FETCH_HEAD` dated today 15:34, `main` tracking `origin/main`. Aviran appears to have configured it today. Correcting in place per Safety rule 9's own "report what the command said, not that you synced."
- **Two `passenger-brain` commits are unpushed** — `origin/main..HEAD` = `30e002f` (this entry) and `1c37640` (`product`, T-034/PAS-25). I did **not** push: `CLAUDE.md` gates *configuring the remote and pushing* on Aviran, and he has evidently done the configuring half only. Whether agents may now push is not stated anywhere, and inferring "yes" from a config change is the workaround-becomes-norm move Safety rule 9 exists to stop. **For `chief-of-staff`/Aviran:** `CLAUDE.md`'s Safety rule 9 and the workspace-root `CLAUDE.md` both still assert "neither repo has a git remote configured (`git remote -v` is empty in both)" — that sentence is now **stale for `passenger-brain`** and needs Aviran's word on whether push is unblocked. `passenger-code` not checked this turn. `PAS-21`/L-015 should be revisited, not closed on my say-so.
- **Tooling note worth a lesson:** `git log --oneline --not --remotes` — the exact proof command `CLAUDE.md` Safety rule 9 recommends — returned **0** here while `git branch -vv` said `ahead 2` and `git log origin/main..HEAD` correctly listed both commits. Anyone using `--not --remotes` alone as their pushed/not-pushed proof will report "nothing unpushed" on a repo with unpushed work. Use `origin/<branch>..HEAD`.

### 2026-07-31 — designer — T-032/PAS-15: additive design pass for the edge gesture — Q5 closed (stays horizontal), Q2 given concrete numbers, Q6 adopted; mockup extended and verified live

- **Provenance:** dispatched with a full context brief naming exactly what to read: `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/agents/designer.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md`'s Current Snapshot plus every 2026-07-31 T-032 worklog entry (two FOUNDER-DIRECT STUBs, `product`'s two PRD amendments to v2/v3, `architect`'s feasibility read), the PRD (Draft v3), the existing approved design spec (v1, 2026-07-30), and the TRD (`2f955fe`). Read all of it before starting rather than re-deriving any of the verbatim quotes or the feasibility findings — this task's brief was explicit that none of that should be re-derived.
- **Did:** produced v2 of `design/phase-1/time-slider-design.md` — an additive pass, not a rewrite. The v1-approved `ZStack` modal card, its nav-row anchoring, and its three exit paths are untouched and not re-justified. New: §1.2 (edge-gesture flow), new rows in §2/§3/§4/§5/§7 for the edge control, a new §5a (Q5 decision) and §5b (Q6 adoption), a new §9 (`ui-design-review` pass, in the required Passes/Issues-found-and-fixed/Quick-wins-considered shape) and a new §10 (Artifact Conformance check, recomputing every contrast figure directly against the republished mockup source rather than citing from memory).
- **Q5 (button/modal axis) — designer's call, per the PRD's own assignment: stays horizontal, unchanged.** Reasoning in full in the spec's §5a: the founder's gesture correction (both verbatim quotes) is about the edge control only, never touches the modal; the compact card's form factor fits a horizontal control and a vertical one would either balloon the card or starve the track; the edge gesture and the button/modal are different physical interaction moments with no PRD line requiring a shared axis; and going vertical would force the modal off its native `Slider` onto a custom control, losing req 6's platform-drawn rail exemption for no requirement-driven reason. Consequence stated explicitly: req 1's widened reachability bullet binds the edge path only (the button's horizontal track was already verified reachable at v1); req 6's rail exemption stays in force on the button (still platform-drawn) and does **not** extend to the new edge track (custom-drawn — held to the full 3:1 bar instead).
- **Q2 (per-idiom edge availability) — concrete numbers given, not just a stated policy.** Both edges live on iPhone; iPad claims the left edge only (right is system Slide Over, rendered with a neutral "OS-reserved" mark rather than a silent dead zone). Usable vertical band: 64pt top inset (clear of Notification/Control Center) / 40pt bottom inset (clear of the home indicator) — 708pt usable on an 812pt reference device, ~59pt per hour-stop, comfortably past the 44pt Fitts minimum. MapKit-pan-arbitration capture band priced at 24pt wide per live edge (half the 44pt touch-target minimum) — visualized directly in the mockup via a toggleable, off-by-default reviewer overlay rather than only asserted as a number in prose. Left explicitly to `architect`'s TRD: the exact iOS gesture-recognizer construction, and confirming the 64pt/40pt insets against real iOS 26 device geometry (design spec §8, items 6–7).
- **Q6 (sheet-open suppression) — adopted, not re-derived.** The PRD is explicit that this is `product`'s own call, made at `architect`'s request, and "cheap to overturn," not a founder answer. Adopted it as the working assumption for the states and mockup, re-confirming (not re-deriving) the three reasons already in the PRD, and recorded one flagged-but-rejected alternative (partial edge availability scoped to a sheet's own footprint) so a reviewer sees the trade-off was considered on purpose.
- **Q3 (one shared value vs. two sliders) — not touched, per the brief's explicit instruction.** Stays open, stays **[ASSUMPTION]**, Aviran's call. The mockup demonstrates one-shared-value behavior (edge drag then button readback shows the identical hour) to make the assumption's practical shape visible, without pretending the question itself is resolved.
- **Mockup:** same artifact URL as v1 (`https://claude.ai/code/artifact/a31d3b48-5500-4eab-b962-f0e12d9f0eea`), extended in place, not re-issued — fetched the live published source first (`WebFetch`), confirmed the exact CSS tokens/hex values already in use so every new contrast citation reuses an already-verified pair rather than inventing one, then edited a local copy and republished with the same `url` parameter. **Verified functionally in a live browser session, not just read from the source:** dragging the left edge zone changes `state.hour` through the identical `setHour()` the horizontal track calls, and opening the heat modal afterward reads back the same value (Q3, demonstrated); direction confirmed up=later; closing to plain map restores the idle hint (opacity 0→0.92, confirmed via computed style, not just a class-toggle assumption); opening any sheet hides the idle hint and makes an edge drag a provable no-op (`state.hour` doesn't move); switching device idiom to iPad disables the right edge (no-op, confirmed) while the left stays live; the reviewer capture-band overlay renders a distinct tint (app-claimed) vs. hatch (OS-reserved) for the two edges.

Fixed by switching the calculation to `#mapArea`'s own rect, re-verified.

### 2026-07-31 — ios-code-reviewer — T-033/PAS-13 `trd-review` (iOS client track, C1-C12), second signature: APPROVE with minor notes

- **Scope:** second-signature buildability/HIG/security read of `hood-place-detail/TRD.md` (as amended through `431b690`) for C1-C12, alongside `ios-developer`'s pass (above) and the already-landed `developer`/`data-engineer` verdicts. Read the TRD, the PRD, and both schema-owning sibling TRDs (`places-dataset/TRD.md` T-042, `hood-dataset/TRD.md` T-040) directly rather than trusting prior summaries.
- **Verdict: APPROVE with minor notes.** No blocking findings. Buildable, correct, internally consistent, with three findings complementary to (not duplicating) `ios-developer`'s pass.
- **Schema re-confirmed independently, 4th time this field-check has been run: every field T-033 reads or is asked to confirm — `id`, `name`, `category`, `hood_id`, `latitude`, `longitude`, `updated_at`, `permanently_closed` — is present in T-042's `places` table (`places-dataset/TRD.md` §3.2), and `hoods.blurb` in T-040's `003_hood_attributes.sql`.** No gap. Agree with `ios-developer`/`developer`/`data-engineer` that `updated_at` exists but is read by nothing in this feature — harmless, noted only for completeness.
- **Agree with `ios-developer`'s `isDepth1Presented`/`isDepth2Presented` finding** (§4.1 doesn't declare the two-way `Binding<Bool>` accessors §4.2's code uses, and a sheet swipe-dismiss needs the setter to route to `closeHood()`/`closePlace()` or the router desyncs from the screen) — independently verified against §4.1/§4.2's actual text, real and worth folding into §4.1 before C5, not blocking.
- **New finding 1 (should-fix, not blocking): the Hood button's z-order/visibility relationship to T-032's nav-surface presentation is unspecified in both TRDs, and it changes whether §4.2's cross-task rule is reachable.** Read `time-slider/TRD.md` §2.3's chrome table directly: while a nav-row surface (e.g. the heat modal) is presented, T-032 puts the scrim at z1 (blocks map taps — so a pin/polygon tap can never open a Hood sheet while a nav surface is up) and explicitly hides+disables hit-testing on "bucket-2 chrome" (`NearMeButton`+`SettingsHint`) at z2, while `MapNavRow` itself stays at z4, always visible and hit-testable. T-033's `HoodButton` (§4.7, D6) is built "to the existing `NearMeButton` chrome idiom... same bottom band" but T-032's z-order table was written without knowing `HoodButton` exists, and T-033's TRD never states which bucket it falls into. Two readings are both defensible and produce different behaviour: if `HoodButton` inherits `NearMeButton`'s bucket-2 treatment (hidden while a nav surface is presented), then §4.2's cross-task rule ("opening a Hood sheet or place modal from the map closes any open nav-row modal first") has **no reachable trigger left** — map taps are already blocked by the scrim, and the Hood button would be hidden too — making the rule dead code as currently scoped. If `HoodButton` is instead exempt like `MapNavRow` (always visible/hit-testable), the rule is real and necessary, and whichever task builds second must wire it exactly as both TRDs already flag. This needs one sentence in `HoodButton`'s spec (§4.7) or T-032's chrome table before C10 is built — cheap, not a redesign, and doesn't block `trd-review` clearing.
- **New finding 2 (should-fix, not blocking): `Place.hoodID`'s population path for the live fetch isn't stated.** §4.3's embedded `GET` shape is `hoods?select=id,blurb,places(id,name,category,latitude,longitude)` — no `hood_id` key appears per place row, because it's structurally implied by nesting under each Hood. But `Place` (§3.2) requires a `hoodID: String` field, and §4.3's boundary-validation table treats "`hood_id` not present in the bundled Hood catalog" as a condition to check against already-parsed `Place` values. Nothing in §3.2/§4.3/§4.4 states that `PlacesAPI`'s decoder must stamp `hoodID` from the enclosing Hood's `id` while walking the embedded response — a different decode path than the flat bundled seed (§3.4), where `hood_id` is presumably a literal column per row. Two structurally different sources feeding one `Decodable` type is fine, but the TRD should say so explicitly rather than leave `ios-developer` to infer it, since guessing wrong here is a silent-miscategorization bug (a place attributed to the wrong Hood), not a crash.
- **New finding 3 (minor, cosmetic): §4.8's reserved T-035 flag-line description reads as live conditional logic** ("this modal renders exactly the string 'Tourist-heavy spot' when flagged and nothing when not") but per D7, `Place.isTouristTrap` is explicitly **not** part of this task's model — that field lands with T-035. Recommend one clause clarifying that C8 ships a genuinely empty reserved slot in V1 (no field to condition on yet), so `ios-developer` doesn't either fabricate a placeholder `isTouristTrap` on `Place` (contradicting D7) or hardcode the line to always-absent in a way that reads as a decision rather than an acknowledged gap.
- **D7 (client-field split) and §11 B2 (export ownership) — ratifying from this pass too, not re-litigating.** Both are already settled on T-042's side (its ratified D5/D4) and confirmed stale-but-harmless by `ios-developer`/`developer`/`data-engineer` above; the fixture-carries-four/model-gains-lazily split is sound Swift practice (`Decodable` ignores unknown keys by default), no objection from the client-engineering side.
- **HIG / App Store / security pass — clean, no findings.** Standard system `.sheet` + detents (no custom chrome masquerading as a system control); `.presentationBackgroundInteraction` is proven first (C1) before any sheet content is built, which is the right order given §9 names it the highest-risk mechanism; touch targets ≥44pt specified at every control (✕, Save, Hood button, empty-state CTA); VoiceOver labels and Dynamic Type explicitly in scope (C11); Reduce Motion referenced for `HoodButton` via the `NearMeButton` idiom; pins staying real `Button`s (not inert annotations) for VoiceOver reachability, with idempotent `openPlace` calls making the resulting double-tap-path safe, is a genuinely good accessibility call, not a hand-wave. Route hand-off uses first-party `MKMapItem`/`openInMaps` — no `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes` added since no `canOpenURL` probe is needed (correctly minimal, avoids an unnecessary declared-API-usage line). No ATT/IDFA surface, no IAP touched. `SavedPlacesStore` is slug-only, no coordinates or timestamps — the one on-device artifact in this feature that could plausibly leak movement, and it's already minimal.
- **security-auditor: agree with `ios-developer`/`developer` — not needed specifically for C1-C12.** This track authors no migration, adds no RLS surface, and its one local-storage artifact is already minimal. Support the standing recommendation (now made 2x) for a lightweight `security-auditor` pass across T-040/T-042/T-043's actual RLS SQL before Aviran applies any of them — the schema this feature's only network read depends on — not gated on T-033 itself.
- **Did not:** write or modify any Swift file, touch any TRD, or touch `BOARD.md`/Linear.
- **Left behind:** the Hood-button bucket-classification gap (finding 1) for `architect` or whoever builds C10/§4.7 to pin in one sentence; the `hoodID` decode-path note (finding 2) for whoever builds C3; the T-035 flag-line phrasing clarification (finding 3) for whoever builds C8; the `isDepth1Presented`/`isDepth2Presented` binding gap, shared with `ios-developer`'s finding, not duplicated in effort.
- **Git:** committed to `passenger-brain` (`main`), explicit path — `agent-os/PROGRESS.md`. Committed, not pushed — no remote configured (workspace root `CLAUDE.md`).
